# 《沉星之序》全角色文本集

> 来源：data/strings/subtitles/en.subtitles + s-cn.subtitles（官方简体中译）。
> 共 585 个段落、5,727 个台词块、31 个说话人标签。英文为原文，CN 行为官方中译；标注"无官方中译"的段落官方简体字幕文件未收录。
> 斜体"导演注释"为字幕文件内嵌的作者备注（非游戏内可见内容）。

---


<!-- ================= 01_queen ================= -->

# 女王 The Queen

> 无名女王，框架叙事的起点（"这里……是来世吗？"）。配音：Emily O’Brien（游戏内演员表自述）。

**统计**：台词块 61 · 英文词数 368 · 涉及段落 23 · 官方中译覆盖 45/61

---


### `intro_queen_am_i_dead`

- **EN** Is this ... the afterlife?
  **CN** 这里……是死后的世界吗？

### `intro_queen_callout_alt`

- **EN** Jackays! Conroe! Are you here?
  **CN** 杰凯斯！康罗！你们在这里吗？
- **EN** Any loyal soldier, stand count!
  **CN** 忠诚的将士们，出来报到！

### `intro_queen_foreign_script`

- **EN** Foreign script, but I understand it, somehow.
  **CN** 异域文字，可不知为何，我竟看得懂。

### `intro_queen_sign`

- **EN** Not too Heavenly, this.
  **CN** 此地可不太像天国。

### `intro_queen_hosts_1`

- **EN** Hosts of this place, show yourselves!
  **CN** 此地的主人，现身吧！
- **EN** Your guest demands it.
  **CN** 你们的客人有令。

### `intro_queen_hosts_2`

- **EN** You who call yourselves Sinking Star!
  **CN** 自称“沉星之序”的诸位！
- **EN** If you want to help me, send me back to my men.
  **CN** 若真想助我，就送我回到部下身边。

### `intro_queen_hosts_3`

- **EN** The raid must be yet ongoing. I've got to find my way back.
  **CN** 突袭想必仍在继续。我必须设法回去。

### `intro_queen_teleporter`

- **EN** Foreign transport, please take me home.
  **CN** 异域的传送装置，请送我回家。

### `queen_og_ask`

- **EN** I suppose that was too much to ask.
  **CN** 看来，是我奢求了。

### `end1_dimension_0`

- **EN** Hail! Hail, who speaks?
  **CN** 喂！是谁在说话？
- **EN** Hello! Where ... where are you?!
  **CN** 有人吗！你们……你们在哪儿？！
- **EN** You who are speaking, make answer! You are Sinking Star?
  **CN** 刚才说话的，回答我！你们就是沉星之序的人？
- **EN** Address me directly! Whence do you speak?
  **CN** 直接回答我！你们究竟身在何处？
  *导演注释：Getting more frustrated, more like a statement or demand.*
- **EN** (sigh)
  **CN** （叹气）

### `end1_dimension_2`

- **EN** Might you please just ... speak with me plainly?
  **CN** 你们能不能……跟我说人话？

### `end3_queen_promesst_red`

- **EN** I cannot account for it... but I have the strange sense that I've been here before.
  **CN** 我说不上来……却总有种奇怪的感觉，好像以前来过这里。

### `queen_og_heroes_entry`

- **EN** These fortifications look stalwart enough.
  **CN** 这些防御工事看着还算坚固。
- **EN** Though ... I had thought thus about my own walls.
  **CN** 不过……我从前对自己的城墙也是这么想的。

### `queen_og_heroes2`

- **EN** Subtle miasma...
  **CN** 隐隐透着一股不祥之气……

### `queen_og_wardrobe`

- **EN** A choice of outfits?
  **CN** 还能挑衣服？

### `queen_og_wardrobe_2`

- **EN** The cloth feels smooth and well-tailored. And I feel like ... a new person.
  **CN** 布料触感光滑，剪裁也很合身。而且我感觉自己仿佛……变了个人。

### `queen_og_first_green_beam`

- **EN** Not sure I'll ever get used to this, not when it's happening to *me*.
  **CN** 不过这种事落到自己身上，我恐怕永远也习惯不了。

### `queen_og_first_orange_beam`

- **EN** Disorienting...
  **CN** 一时竟不知自己身在何处……

### `queen_og_first_lily`

- **EN** Well, that was a cute ride.
  **CN** 这一程倒颇为别致。

### `queen_og_mirror_isles`

- **EN** An air of contemplation...
  **CN** 一股凝神沉思的氛围……

### `queen_og_gold_room_first`

- **EN** Seems like a place they want me to notice.
  **CN** 看来，他们是想让我留意这里。

### `audiolog_di_tiger_lily_v2`

- **EN** 
  **CN** 我是您虔诚的门徒，继承了您的使命……
- **EN** 
  **CN** 若我有幸活过今日，我定会守护您的这些圣物，
  *导演注释：This is a very emotional time. They have succeeded in finding some very sacred relics, but at the same time, they're about to be attacked by a superior force, and she knows that her and all her closest companions are likely to die in the next hour.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这些我们寻觅了那么久的圣物……愿它们不再遗失，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 愿它们展现在世人面前，成为指引方向的明灯，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 让您的形象留在百姓心中，燃起希望、激励人心， 我将引领他们重获新生。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但若我注定无法离开此地； 若前路只会通向那深渊巨口，就让我独自前行吧；
  *导演注释：This sounds positive, but it's more like a positive image she *used* to hold that she has given up on today since the situation seems impossible.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 别让我的部下与我一同入土。 请保护他们，他们的忠诚应当得到您的回报，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 让他们平安度过今日，回到孩子与妻子身边。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 他们会把我和您一同铭记， 我们的传奇故事或许仍能照亮未来，造福世人。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可我……
  *导演注释：Actually some hope here when talking about the future that she is no longer in.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 什么？！出什么事了？！
- **EN** 
  **CN** ……
  *导演注释：Very irritated as he's broken her out of the trance, and also, she doesn't want to be seen in this emotional state*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这里……是死后的世界吗？
  *导演注释：Turning back to where the Queen was...*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 过了多久？
  *导演注释：He can't believe his eyes; she has disappeared. He's looking around the nearby rocks and bushes.... surely she's here somewhere? Did that just-shot arrow somehow make her magically disappear? "What dark enchantment" is to self; the part after is calling out.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 行，你可以接着汇报了。
  *导演注释：(he rushes with her to a spot behind a big fallen tree trunk)*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你！

### `end3_suit` （无官方中译）

- **EN** I am your ardent disciple, heir to your charge...
- **EN** If it be granted me to survive this day, I will guard these your relics
  *导演注释：This is a very emotional time. They have succeeded in finding some very sacred relics, but at the same time, they're about to be attacked by a superior force, and she knows that her and all her closest companions are likely to die in the next hour.*
- **EN** for which we have searched *so long*... let them not be lost,
- **EN** let them be set forth for all to see, a beacon;
- **EN** and with your image in our people's hearts, stoking hope and inspiration, I will lead them forth into renewal.
- **EN** But if I am never to leave this place, if the road leads only into the Maw, let me go alone;
  *导演注释：This sounds positive, but it's more like a positive image she *used* to hold that she has given up on today since the situation seems impossible.*
- **EN** let my men be not buried with me. Protect them, whom you must reward for their faithfulness,
- **EN** and bring them safely through this day, that they may return to their chidren and their wives.
- **EN** They will remember me alongside you, and our legend may yet shine into the future and do much good.
- **EN** But I --
  *导演注释：Actually some hope here when talking about the future that she is no longer in.*
- **EN** What??? What is it???
- **EN** ...
  *导演注释：Very irritated as he's broken her out of the trance, and also, she doesn't want to be seen in this emotional state*
- **EN** Is this... the afterlife?
  *导演注释：Turning back to where the Queen was...*
- **EN** How long has it been?
  *导演注释：He can't believe his eyes; she has disappeared. He's looking around the nearby rocks and bushes.... surely she's here somewhere? Did that just-shot arrow somehow make her magically disappear? "What dark enchantment" is to self; the part after is calling out.*
- **EN** Well then, you may finish your report.
  *导演注释：(he rushes with her to a spot behind a big fallen tree trunk)*
- **EN** You!


<!-- ================= 02_director ================= -->

# 总监 The Director

> 沉星会正任总监，出门"调查"多年未归；在 audiolog_u_* 系列亲自面试 Leamington。片尾 Milton《失乐园》祈愿由 jon（疑为 Jonathan Blow 本人）朗读，与此角呼应。

**统计**：台词块 1136 · 英文词数 8714 · 涉及段落 47 · 官方中译覆盖 672/1136

---


### `queen_og_gold_room_first`

- **EN** 
  **CN** 和大多数地方的人一样，我们最初玩的游戏也很简单， 用的是纸牌、小木人之类的东西，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 再定下各种规矩：各张纸牌的含义， 木人能走的位置，走到了会怎样。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 随着技术不断进步，我们的游戏也随之发展。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我知道，你们有一种齿轮，既能相互咬合，也能相互脱离。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你设想一下，有一种特殊齿轮，每秒能旋转数十亿圈。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这些齿轮非常微小，你既听不见，也看不到。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 把十亿枚这样的齿轮组合起来，让它们不停地咬合、脱开，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你就能给自己的游戏搭出一套相当讲究的规则。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 玩家不再需要自己执行规则——
- **EN** 
  **CN** 齿轮会负责运算逻辑，模拟各种物体以及它们之间的互动，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这样，游戏规则就可以变得极其复杂、环环相扣， 复杂到人根本无法手工完成运算。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这种系统问世后，能创造出来的游戏一下多了无数种， 远非纸牌和木人的年代可比。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 那个时代的人还制造了特殊屏幕， 让他们得以窥见那些小小的游戏世界——
- **EN** 
  **CN** “视频”屏幕——于是“电子游戏”这种媒介应运而生，并兴盛了一段时间。

### `audiolog_u_old_games` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Like most cultures, we started with primitive games -- objects like cards, or little wooden men,
- **EN** and rules about what the cards mean, where the men can go and what happens as a result.
- **EN** As our technology advanced, so did our games.
- **EN** I know that you have gears that can shift into and out of conjunction.
- **EN** Imagine you had very special gears, spinning at billions of revolutions per second.
- **EN** These gears are very small, you could neither hear nor see them.
- **EN** You put together a billion such, shifting into and out of relation,
- **EN** and you can develop quite a system of rules for your game.
- **EN** The players no longer have to enforce the rules themselves --
- **EN** the gears perform the logic, representing the objects and their interactions,
- **EN** and now you can make games whose rules are far too complex and interdependent to administer by hand.
- **EN** When this kind of system was invented, the space of games that could be created suddenly became vastly larger, compared to those days of cards and wooden men.
- **EN** The people of that time made special screens that allowed them to see into those little game worlds --
- **EN** "video" screens -- and thus the medium of "video games" was born, and flourished for a while.
- **EN** As our technology advanced, so did our games. We always brought games with us,
- **EN** through the upheavals and calamities that we were lucky to survive -- *seemed* lucky to survive.
- **EN** What we do today, in these Warehouses, you couldn't call the same thing.
  *导演注释："seemed lucky to survive" does not refer to a negative thing (sucks that we lived), rather, a mysterious and positive thing (it seems like maybe there was divine intervention helping us out, so we weren't strictly "lucky").*
- **EN** But it has its roots in the games of our elders,
- **EN** and our Order holds this time in great respect.
- **EN** Occasionally we look back and re-experience the works of that time, as best we can,
- **EN** to return to a childlike state, to try and notice what we've lost in the modern day.
- **EN** And sometimes, we do find litle nuggets of gold.

### `audiolog_u_wizard_mirror_worlds_connect`

- **EN** Though I hope for you to be a good keeper of this warehouse,
  **CN** 我固然希望你能把这座仓库照管好，
- **EN** I always want people thinking of the next level up, also.
  **CN** 但我始终希望大家能考虑再高一个层次的问题。
- **EN** So here's something to consider as you observe, review, and maintain.
  **CN** 所以在你考察、复核和维护时，不妨思考一件事。
- **EN** When we merge different worlds, they don't connect to each other automatically.
  **CN** 将不同世界拼合在一起时，它们之间并不会自动产生互动。
- **EN** We must add cement between, to help them join; choosing the composition of that cement is our opportunity to exert authorial voice.
  **CN** 我们得补上让不同世界相互作用的规则，把它们黏合起来； 而这些规则怎么定，就体现了我们的创作风格。
- **EN** For example: You have the Wizard. In his own world, he is well-defined: He teleports around in such-and-such a way.
  **CN** 举个例子：这里有法师。在他自己的世界里， 他的一切都有明确而完整的定义：他以某种特定的方式四处传送。
- **EN** You have the magic Mirror. In its own world, it is well-defined.
  **CN** 这里有一面魔镜。在它原本的世界里，它有一套明确的运行规则。
- **EN** Now the worlds come together. How is the Wizard to interact with the Mirror?
  **CN** 现在，两个世界合而为一。法师该如何与镜子互动？
- **EN** You have several choices.
  **CN** 你有几种选择。
- **EN** You could say, the Mirror behaves as any old Stone, so the Wizard just swaps with it.
  **CN** 你可以设计成，让镜子跟普通石头一样， 法师只需与它交换位置。
- **EN** Simple. Clean. But boring, and irresponsible:
  **CN** 简单，利落，但也显得无聊和敷衍：
- **EN** there's an eddy of potential there that's been ignored:
  **CN** 因为镜子还有不少潜力可以挖掘，却被这种做法忽略了：
- **EN** the Mirror has a shape. It's reflective in two directions.
  **CN** 镜子有自己的形状，而且能朝两个方向反射。
- **EN** What *should* this mean, with respect to the Wizard and the way he moves?
  **CN** 对法师及其移动方式而言，这理应意味着什么？
- **EN** As an Implementor, you would get to decide.
  **CN** 身为构建者，决定权在你手中。
- **EN** Your decision may create new, additional vortices of behavior
  **CN** 你的决定还可能催生更多全新的互动方式，
- **EN** that are delightful for a Visitor to explore.
  **CN** 让访客探索起来其乐无穷。
  *导演注释：Todo: Audio skips the "And" - Charles*
- **EN** Or, if your decision is not so good, the players may just find it dull, boring, contrived.
  **CN** 或者，如果你的决定不够好， 玩家只会觉得乏味、无聊，一眼就看出是刻意设计的。
- **EN** As you review your holdings, I'd like you to observe:
  **CN** 在你检查你所掌管的这些作品时，我希望你留心观察：
- **EN** what are these choices that were made filling the gaps between the worlds,
  **CN** 当初为了填补不同世界之间的空白，设计者做出了哪些选择；
- **EN** and what do you, personally, think of those choices?
  **CN** 而你自己，又如何看待这些选择？
- **EN** Would you have done it differently?
  **CN** 你会不会有不同的做法？
- **EN** But really think it through, because it's easy just to say yes there, "Of course, *my* version would be better!"
  **CN** 但你得真正想清楚，因为你很容易脱口而出： “会啊，我的版本当然更好。”
- **EN** Sometimes you'd be right.
  **CN** 有时你确实是对的。
- **EN** But you must always really think it through.
  **CN** 但无论如何，都必须彻底想清楚。
- **EN** The Tetratetrad was originally Lemur's idea, but he prefers to keep his spindly hands off of day-to-day matters.
- **EN** So, I must admit a decent amount of culpability.
  *导演注释：Lemur is the founder and the top of the hierarchy at OSS.*
- **EN** I'll tell you the project was ridiculous beyond even what you've heard,
- **EN** because the worlds inside each tetrad were chosen for maximum density —
- **EN** with an unusual number of affordances, higher even than any of the worlds in this Warehouse.
- **EN** More affordances per world means, when the worlds are connected, there are tremendously more arcs in the graph.
- **EN** This tangle of arcs becomes very difficult to wrangle,
- **EN** but it's the whole reason we're here, So we had to just eat the problem.
- **EN** Once we had chosen some candidate worlds, we kept Tetrising the tetrads
- **EN** to achieve maximum volume of the resulting product space. This was an iterative process.
- **EN** Once things were drafted out, and we had a full system that we could operate and test,
- **EN** we would often see opportunities to do better if we substituted some new world for an existing one.
- **EN** Similarly for entire tetrads. We had a farm league of candidate tetrads that we had roughed out to various levels of completion,
- **EN** and several of these drafts were swapped in and out of the four tetrad slots.
- **EN** We continued to find new ways to pump up the density --
- **EN** this meant we had engaged in a fair bit of wasted work,
- **EN** as Implementors might work with a particular world for years, only to find it swapped out one day. Poof.
- **EN** It's very easy to feel mistreated, and become frustrated.
  *导演注释：Todo: Double occurrence of the word 'work'.*
  *导演注释：Todo: work with a particular world for years, developing the connective tissue with its neighbors,*
- **EN** But we all know why we're here: it's all to the glory of God.
  *导演注释：Todo: Cut "become frustrated".*
- **EN** So most of us got over our issues, and if you were open to it, it wasn't so hard to see the bright side:
- **EN** "Hey, if your pet world didn't make it in, then after the four-four is done,
- **EN** you can use that world in a different combiner. Your work won't be completely wasted. It's fine.
  *导演注释：Todo: Cut this quote.*
- **EN** The real product here is the refinement of your soul,
- **EN** not the object we construct, which is temporary, like all Earthly things."
- **EN** We encouraged Implementors to accept this kind of reasoning, to help them remain sane, to avoid everything blowing up.
- **EN** ... you see, it just wasn't good enough to curate a single beautiful world;
- **EN** we were solving a global optimization problem containing many parts that interacted across multiple levels of abstraction.
- **EN** To improve the mix, one could:
- **EN** (a) Revise the selection of dynamics we borrow from a particular world: which people, which objects?
- **EN** (b) Revise the rules that define the dynamics between foreign objects from different worlds
- **EN** (lacking these rules, no possibility would be defined, so the objects could not interact at all)
- **EN** (c) Substitute an entire world inside a tetrad, to achieve maximum cross-connectivity between the four worlds;
- **EN** (d) Substitute an entire tetrad within the Tetratetrad;
- **EN** ... And all the while, adapting all of the ten thousand individual puzzles
- **EN** a Visitor would face -- because when you change the macro-scale shape of the whole thing,
- **EN** this adds pressure in some places, relieves it in others, and the optimal form of any individual puzzle changes in response.
- **EN** Because here's the thing: we're powerful, but we aren't magic.
- **EN** We don't know how well a world, or an entire tetrad, is going to function, until we put it together and observe the outcome;
- **EN** then we can work hard to iterate and push it further, again and again,
- **EN** seeing how good we can make it in the limit, so we can then ask: Is this new idea truly better than what we had before?
- **EN** Or should I scrap all this work I just did?
- **EN** For us, the process of answering these questions can take years.
- **EN** Puzzles were constantly being created, refined, discarded. Even the best of them.
- **EN** Very beautiful puzzles were regularly dropped into Cold Storage, which was heartbreaking,
- **EN** but it had to happen because the environment for which they were designed no longer existed,
- **EN** and under the new arrangement they felt clumsy, incomplete, unsatisfying.
- **EN** The Tetratetrad was certainly not the best place for a Visitor's first exposure to our work -- too dense, too hard to follow --
- **EN** but if they'd done a good ten or twenty other Warehouses first, they could work their way up to it.
- **EN** For us, it was the brightest star in our crown. Or it would be, when finished.
- **EN** The decay is eating into our Warehouses as though they were Swiss cheese.
- **EN** And by studying that cheese, we hope to learn
- **EN** how to protect the facilities we've built across these dozen decades, all seventy thousand of them,
- **EN** so that the whole thing doesn't just shrivel and die.
- **EN** So far a solution has eluded us. We're performing deep research in the background to figure this out.
- **EN** And one day we may; meanwhile, we have to stay afloat: just slow the rot. Beat it back. Preserve.
- **EN** My primary task: discover what we don't yet understand
- **EN** about the laws of the Ultimate Ensemble, and the related holography.
- **EN** The audacity of the goal is invigorating to many.
- **EN** It's our new tetratetrad, but better, because it involves the potential for discovery of new fundamental laws.
- **EN** My team has made some strides -- small strides, but they represent
- **EN** the first addition to that area of Natural Philosophy in 200 years.
- **EN** We may learn to cleanse the decay. We may learn to build space made of stronger stuff.
- **EN** In the meantime, we need to isolate and fortify the areas proximal to the rot, *this* Warehouse being one of the worst.
- **EN** We know that our problems have to do with the connectedness of the whole --
- **EN** the whole of all space configured by our Order, together, space which is causally intertwined by its connective arcs.
- **EN** Somehow, the cross-correlation has become too dense.
- **EN** Expanding the volume of the space doesn't help us any more -- diminishing returns,
- **EN** like there is a kind of super-information, or super-causation, we don't yet understand.
- **EN** Classical holography has an information density limit, that you measure in Planck-area units,
- **EN** but here we face a tighter constraint -- something much worse, that kicks in for causally heterogeneous spaces.
- **EN** Some think the bounds involve consciousness in some way, which I agree is possible,
- **EN** given the way our projections interact with awareness via the Hook channels.
- **EN** We've only just realized that fresh observers help with preservation:
- **EN** those who have never before experienced a particular Warehouse,
  *导演注释：Todo: only just -> only recently*
- **EN** when it's all new to them, somehow their activities strengthen the projections for a while.
- **EN** So you, unexpected Visitor, have helped us a bit already,
- **EN** and there are a few more Warehouses I'd like you to visit...
- **EN** We are reclusive these days. The Lifeboat Societies were already spare in their interactions --
- **EN** this is baked into our cultures at their moments of founding.
- **EN** The decay has made our Order ... even solitarier.
  *导演注释：by their moments of founding.*
- **EN** If we stay connected to some other party, they become part of our causal cluster for that interval;
- **EN** the increased density of the enlarged cluster accelerates the decay.
- **EN** It's so funny. In our physical Ensemble, look at the incredible
- **EN** amount of stuff that exists without any trouble: all those galaxies you see when looking into the sky, and all the incredible space beyond.
  *导演注释：Todo: funny?*
- **EN** However God built the ceiling of that cathedral is still a mystery; our Order's ceiling is lower, and lacks vaulting.
- **EN** To slow the decay, we have severed connection not just with outsiders, but also internally --
- **EN** for example, the creations of the Static and Dynamic Brigades are firewalled from each other.
- **EN** Some went off to start a disjoint colony sending only rare and cryptic communications — a low-density restart.
- **EN** I wish them well and hope someday to see what they have achieved.
- **EN** Given this degree of isolation, you can understand why there was a kerfuffle
- **EN** among the Preservation Crew when you just *showed up*.
  *导演注释：After a pause.*
- **EN** But ... ... what they don't know won't hurt them.
- **EN** Some say we should destroy our older Warehouses to make room for new creations.
- **EN** I find the very idea distasteful! And, I don't think it would work.
- **EN** Even should we denature everything here, down to ash ...
- **EN** well, it is the case that innumerable specks of dust look interchangeable to us humans,
- **EN** but God knows the history and personal character of each and every one.
- **EN** The amount of information never goes down.
- **EN** All that happens is, when you burn things, your personal ignorance goes up.
- **EN** The apparent distance between you and God grows.

### `audiolog_u_chaos_1` （无官方中译）

- **EN** You're an engineer; you try to understand the order in the world
- **EN** so you can exploit it to build complicated things; isn't that so?
- **EN** You started by saying "chaos" -- perhaps meaning cause-and-effect
- **EN** that proceeds through too many steps, too quickly, for our human brains to follow --
- **EN** and then you call this "randomness", meaning things that are arbitrary and have no meaning.
- **EN** I would say these are improper words...

### `audiolog_u_chaos_2` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Yeah, you're making a face. You haven't yet achieved the arts of information processing in your world, then?
  *导演注释："the maximum amount of information that could He is letting the sentence dwindle and die out... he's not sure that this question is even the right question, as he's asking it.*
- **EN** Push the button on your recorder there, so that you get down these words in my native language,
- **EN** and your assignment will be to use the library -- we'll get someone to show you the library, it's really neat actually! --
- **EN** to research anything you need, in depth, using these specific words to ensure accuracy, until you understand these statements:
- **EN** Information can be represented by a certain number of bits.
- **EN** For any fixed number of bits -- say you have a certain amount of physical space in which to write, one symbol per box --
- **EN** there's a limit to the amount of information the symbols can hold.
- **EN** If you just naively write facts into those bits, you will run out of space, falling woefully short of the potential that you *could* have reached.
- **EN** But you can improve the amount of information you can fit, by finding shorter ways to write equivalent sentences.
- **EN** If there's a long word, you could just stop writing as soon as it's unambiguous; instead of "armchair", you stop after "armch", and rely on your reader to complete the word.
- **EN** If there are frequent long sequences of words, replace those with a few letters. If you find you write "it's a lovely morning" very often,
  *导演注释：Going into some specific examples.*
- **EN** replace this with "kzq", and instruct the reader on this substitution.
- **EN** So you start compressing the facts, finding shorter ways to write, so you can fit more facts into the space.
- **EN** The methods of compression become quite sophisticated. The more you compress, the harder your message is to read --
  *导演注释：Shifting back to the general explanation.*
- **EN** because anyone wishing to understand it needs to perform the inverse operation to decompress.
- **EN** And the more you compress, the more difficult it is to find ways to compress further.
- **EN** Near the limit, improving the compression requires a tremendous amount of effort --
- **EN** one might say a Godly amount of effort -- to make any further gains.
- **EN** Until, finally, you have achieved perfection -- the message cannot possibly be made smaller, and the result you have encoded,
- **EN** to the untrained eye, appears as completely random noise.
- **EN** Now let's look at another scenario. One day, you find a slip of paper with some series of numbers written down;
- **EN** you perform some statistical tests, and it seems completely random insofar as you can tell... well, you still don't know its actual nature.
  *导演注释：Shifting gears to come at it a different way.*
- **EN** Is it random, and thus you might call it completely meaningless?
- **EN** Or is it a well-compressed message, with potentially a very deep meaning?
- **EN** Both of these things appear maximally entropic, so you don't know. You *can't* know.
- **EN** Same with an enciphered message. In the beginning, when developing the art of encipherment, you only know naive ways of obscuring what you wrote;
- **EN** a clever spy can study the structure that remains in your encoded message, and work out the rules of encipherment, and thereby read the message.
  *导演注释：Switching to another new topic.*
- **EN** But as you become better at encipherment, this conspicuous structure disappears,
- **EN** and the message becomes nearly impossible to crack. Because it looks exactly like randomness.
- **EN** A well-enciphered message, looks almost exactly like a well-compressed message, looks almost exactly like randomness.
- **EN** So which is it? Which of these three models best represents the Universe in which we live?
  *导演注释：In summary*
- **EN** Enciphered, compressed, or random?
- **EN** I realize this doesn't mean much to you yet. But it's worth learning, even if only as a guiding image.
- **EN** So. Any time that life looks scrambled, or chaotic, or meaningless...
- **EN** It may just be that you lack the decipherment key.

### `audiolog_di_catching_up_v0` （无官方中译）

- **EN** This was our initial design for this puzzle. We decided it was too difficult,
- **EN** so we revised it to be easier (and you'll see that revision next).
- **EN** What's better or worse is always contextual.
- **EN** In a short game, this version might be better than the revision, because it's more mysterious and difficult.
- **EN** Many designers and players would tell you that some of their most memorable moments
- **EN** happened when a challenge looked completely impossible: you enter the level, you see it, it's simple, there's obviously no solution.
- **EN** Then you notice one little thing, and it breaks the whole puzzle open.
- **EN** If you haven't been spoiled already, spend some time solving this before listening to the second recording...

### `audiolog_di_catching_up_v0_part_2` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Over the course of development, we gained a lot of clarity about difficulty: when a puzzle is hard, it could be hard in a good way, or hard in a bad way.
- **EN** Suppose a puzzle is very difficult straight off. You enter the level, you look at it,
- **EN** and it just doesn't give you any idea how to get started.
- **EN** That can be okay in isolation, but when this happens puzzle after puzzle after puzzle in a long game,
- **EN** you're having the same experience over and over -- the experience of being stuck and confused.
- **EN** In a sense, they are all kind of the same puzzle, regardless of their specifics.
- **EN** This becomes tedious. You are working hard with very little reward;
- **EN** it's just like banging your head against a brick wall. So you get tired and maybe you want to quit.
- **EN** That's not a great experience for you, but for *us*, that's not actually the worst part.
- **EN** The worst part is, because you spent almost all your time just trying random ideas, with no clear focus,
- **EN** your thoughtspace is cluttered, and it's hard to see the core ideas that these levels were trying to communicate.
- **EN** If on a typical level you spend an hour just trying random stuff,
- **EN** and only understand what you're meant to do in the last 5 minutes,
- **EN** then only a small fraction of your experience is spent seeing and appreciating the neat ideas that are supposed to be the value delivered by the game.
- **EN** Early in development, we thought we were making puzzles better by maximizing
- **EN** the amount of manipulation you had to do with objects in a level;
- **EN** but over time we became more discerning about difficulty, and I sought to remove this kind of grindy excess Sokobanning.
- **EN** We want you to be spending time and energy on things that are unique to this game.
- **EN** You'll see how we improved the puzzle by changing it drastically in the revised version.

### `audiolog_di_catching_up_v2` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Here, we've redesigned the level so it's immediately obvious that you need to make a stack.
- **EN** There's no other way to get the other guy to the exit.
- **EN** So, the only real question is how to make the stack; if you get stuck for a while figuring that out, that's okay,
- **EN** because you've been thinking about making a stack, and that's the idea that we want you to wrangle with here.
- **EN** Making it clear that you need to make a stack causes the puzzle to be easier, but in a long game,
- **EN** this helps you flow through the puzzles more quickly, one after another;
  *导演注释：shifting gears*
- **EN** this way you can see the flow of ideas better, because the ideas are more concentrated in time.
- **EN** This thing I just said is part of a non-obvious way of thinking about puzzles, so I'll make an analogy:
- **EN** Imagine listening to your favorite song -- the musical vibrations go into your ear, your brain appreciates the melody.
- **EN** If you slow that song down by a factor of two, maybe it's not so enjoyable any more; the tempo is off, the pitches of the notes are weird.
- **EN** But let's slow it down much more, by a factor of 20; at that point, it doesn't even work as music any more.
- **EN** It might be hard to hear anything at all, and to the extent you do, it's just weird low vibrations.
- **EN** Your brain can no longer hear the fun changes between notes in a musical way. You certainly wouldn't get into a groove.
- **EN** There's a particular range of time scales in which things are understandable and feel good.
- **EN** Now apply that to feeling the ideas shown to you by puzzles, as those ideas float invisibly in the space around you.

### `audiolog_di_heroes_1_7` （无官方中译）

- **EN** This is Jonah's original design for one of the earliest Thief levels.
- **EN** The idea is that the Thief can only pull a Crystal onto the Button
- **EN** by going one specific way, and once she gets it there,
- **EN** she can't help but pull the Crystal back off the Button,
- **EN** shutting the Gate once again. Which is funny.
- **EN** You can also make the observation that the regular columns are a difficult structure for the Thief,
- **EN** because she can't ever get the crystal around one of those corners:
- **EN** she can only turn in the intersections,
- **EN** so she always has to leave the crystal at the side of a column.
- **EN** Here, at the beginning of the level, if she pulls the crystal any way but North, she's stuck.
- **EN** If I look through a pair of Design Elegance Goggles, the northwest area of the map looks pretty awkward; it isn't really used,
- **EN** except to suggest the continuation of the grid.

### `audiolog_di_heroes_1_7_v2` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Here's the way I revised Jonah's idea into a completely different level.
- **EN** It's much bigger, an atypical size for early Heroes, but I liked it so I kept it that way.
- **EN** My goal was to give the player more practice using the Thief, and internalizing how she moves,
- **EN** while also making a level that was easier to see the point of --
- **EN** there was something about the original version that never left any meaningful takeaway in my brain.
- **EN** Because this is an early training level, I wanted to use shapes that you see often throughout the game --
- **EN** the columns in Jonah's version just aren't something you see much.
- **EN** So, here we have a series of rooms connected by halls.
- **EN** We maintain the funny situation with the button,
- **EN** but we emphasize it by starting the Thief right next to the button (she's likely to walk onto it as her first move),
- **EN** and placing the crystal far away. It's easy to walk to the crystal,
- **EN** but not so simple to drag it back through the rooms.
- **EN** The rooms are a little bit ridiculous in terms of the number of detours required
- **EN** just to get the crystal where you want it.
- **EN** This design is me having fun with the Thief's movement patterns;
- **EN** but you could also say that the map of the level is *an illustration* of the Thief's movement patterns,
- **EN** in that the room borders show a boundary that maximizes interest.
- **EN** In this version of the level, we abandoned the original idea about columns and corners.
- **EN** Instead, it's about walls: the Thief cannot ever pull an object up to a wall,
- **EN** and this is part of what makes things silly.

### `audiolog_di_strategic_stack` （无官方中译）

- **EN** This is Zach's original version. It's a neat idea for a level,
- **EN** but the geometry doesn't help you see the idea at all.
- **EN** The path to the goal island is indirect and very hard to visualize...
- **EN** and you need to have some inkling of how to reach the goal
- **EN** before you can start engaging with the core idea, which is about using a Mirror in a place
- **EN** that helps you in the beginning, but later blocks you.
- **EN** So, the lack of clarity in reading this level makes it difficult and frustrating to begin with. But then, on top of that, add this:
- **EN** With 3 Mirrors and a Crystal, the natural impulse is to clone the Crystal a bunch of times, which doesn't actually help you here.
- **EN** Indeed, it may seem that the level is *telling you* to clone the crystal, since you've seen other levels that tell you to clone crystals.
- **EN** So not only is the correct pathway buried in obscurity, but the level is actively misleading you.
- **EN** So if a player spends most of his time here just being lost and confused, it's no surprise.

### `audiolog_di_triple_gate` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Here's Patrick's revision. The core idea is similar (teleporting a crystal out from beneath a Mirror),
- **EN** but the reason why you do it is totally different;
- **EN** and, here, you do it twice, which feels better
- **EN** and helps to cement the idea in your mind.
- **EN** Most importantly, unlike the original version, it's very obvious what route you're trying to take,
- **EN** so the Player spends no time being confused about that.
- **EN** He just knows that he has to get past the Gates somehow.

### `audiolog_di_torcher` （无官方中译）

- **EN** This was conceived as a level about using a Mirror to reorient a dragon;
- **EN** in order to roast Goblins in all 4 cardinal directions.
- **EN** Because this was designed back when we felt we had to make levels generally difficult,
- **EN** and require careful Sokoban moves, the area you're trapped inside is fairly tight:
- **EN** to solve the puzzle, you need to do some careful repositioning.
- **EN** Later, after we had built up our aesthetic about when difficulty is worthwhile and productive,
- **EN** I felt this level was too cramped.
- **EN** It's frustrating because you keep killing yourself
- **EN** by stepping in the line of the Dragon's breath (usually when it's reflected off the Mirror, where you see no warning).
- **EN** In such a small space, this becomes hard to avoid,
- **EN** so a significant fraction of your moves are tweaky pre-positioning,
- **EN** so you can then push the Mirror where you want it without dying.
- **EN** In the revisions of this level, you'll see that we made the central chamber bigger;
- **EN** that's one of the main reasons this level is presented here,
- **EN** to show how the amount of space affects playability.
- **EN** Besides being too cramped, there are at least two other serious flaws with this design.
- **EN** Firstly, it's easy to mirror the Goblins into the chamber, or to a different side of the perimeter;
- **EN** so if a player has an unclear idea of the puzzle, and is just experimenting, it's natural for him to make an un-fun mess.
- **EN** It also means you don't actually need to shoot fire in all 4 directions,
- **EN** because you can re-use some directions by teleporting the Goblins,
- **EN** and this damages the core idea: We want all four directions!
- **EN** The second problem is that the "surprise last step" of killing the 5th Goblin
- **EN** is pretty hard to figure out, and that defocuses the level,
- **EN** because you spend all this mental effort on something that does not involve shooting fire in four directions.
- **EN** It's a good ending in the sense that you need to figure out something very specific,
- **EN** but it's also somewhat arbitrary; yes, it is about orienting the dragon in a specific way,
- **EN** but this just doesn't feel very connected to what you did previously.
- **EN** Because this ending is hard,
- **EN** it can become much larger in your memory than the thing we wanted you to remember.

### `audiolog_di_quad_torch_v1` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Here, we've gone from a 5x5 central chamber to 7x7. This makes the main part of the puzzle easier, and perhaps here it's too easy.
- **EN** To me it seemed overly straightforward; as the Player, I just felt
- **EN** "Okay, I did the steps you told me to, therefore I won the level. So what?"
- **EN** We want to avoid that feeling whenever we can, because this is a game, not a homework assignment.
- **EN** Due to the solid outer wall, it's not as easy to mirror the Goblins, so you can no longer make a complete mess;
- **EN** but you can clear one of the islands on the east or south
- **EN** and then mirror the other Goblin onto that island,
- **EN** so the basic problem still exists --
- **EN** but the situation is improved, because you can't mirror Goblins to arbitrary messy places in the middle.
- **EN** This version also has a "surprise last step" of drowning the dragon,
- **EN** but it's easy to figure out, in contrast to the earlier revision;
- **EN** so it feels more like a satisfying piece of ending punctuation than a difficult task that takes over the level.

### `audiolog_di_quad_torch` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Here we have another 7x7 chamber, but now it has a wall in the middle.
- **EN** The wall takes up a little bit of slack space, but it also gives you another way
- **EN** to cross the line of dragon breath when you want to.
- **EN** This means the puzzle is less about crossing dragon breath,
- **EN** and more about aiming and positioning the dragon.
- **EN** You don't have to do a bunch of excess Sokobanning
- **EN** to deal with the fact that you are crammed in.
- **EN** The central column also helps solve the Goblin teleportation problem.
- **EN** The Goblins on the east and south are set back,
- **EN** so you can't teleport them into the chamber; if you wanted to teleport one Goblin to the other's spot,
- **EN** as you could in the previous revision, you would have to place the Mirror where the central column is.
- **EN** And of course you can't do that.
- **EN** So, in this version, you do *finally* have to shoot fire in all 4 directions.
- **EN** You could argue that the "kill the final monster" surprise here is a little off-topic,
- **EN** but it does involve teleporting the dragon to the right place in the right orientation,
- **EN** then escaping the cage, which is fun.
- **EN** And it's tuned to be easier than the original version of the level.
- **EN** The core idea of this level is to set up a situation with an Endpoint Beam and magic Mirror
- **EN** such that you can choose how high you want to go up, teleport to that height,
- **EN** and be held aloft by the Beam so that you can step onto the platform.
- **EN** But there's a lot of other stuff going on. Having to fill the far endpoint of the purple Beam;
- **EN** only being able to use the Lily once to go to the far shore;
- **EN** the ability to recall the skipping stone with the magic Mirror,
- **EN** and maybe change its direction of movement while it's skipping;
- **EN** questions about whether you are supposed to somehow use the Lily more than once,
- **EN** whether you are supposed to sink the skipping stone to make terrain, and so forth.
- **EN** It's just too much uncertainty about what's going on, in a place where the core scenario is already complicated and hard to see.
- **EN** This is the version we ended up going with. It's the same basic idea, but we simplified it:
- **EN** the concepts of Water, Skipping, and Lily have all been removed.
- **EN** You have to set up the Crystal to block the Beam,
- **EN** but the way you do this is more interesting, and it's actually on-topic for the level.
- **EN** By putting the purple Beam on the far side of the level, instead of on top,
- **EN** we make it easier to return to that side and move it, which reduces the amount of grindy Sokobanning.
- **EN** This version is easier, but also better, because you are much more focused on the core idea.
- **EN** This level is included in this collection to show the kind of improvement you can make when simplifying a level.
- **EN** In the original version, certain design subtasks needed to be accomplished: you need to fill the purple endpoint in order to float.
- **EN** But the way we implemented those subtasks created too much confusion for the player.
- **EN** In general, whenever you design a puzzle and it's not as clean as you'd like,
- **EN** you can look at individual subtasks and ask whether you *really* needed to do that thing in that specific way,
- **EN** or whether it can be done in a cleaner way.

### `audiolog_di_push_down_a_lot` （无官方中译）

- **EN** The idea here is, you need to set up these stones so that, once you drop down,
- **EN** you'll be able to push them onto the buttons ...
- **EN** but due to tricky button positioning and the shape of the plateau,
- **EN** it's difficult to see how to cover all the buttons at the same time.
- **EN** This level is divided into two distinct parts,
- **EN** a setup phase and a button-pushing phase;
- **EN** this means it's mostly about pre-planning, because once you drop down, you can't change your crystal placement.
- **EN** So, the level feels like a big mental maze where you are looking
- **EN** at the shape of the plateau and what you can do with it.
- **EN** I like this level a lot; it calls explicit attention to the process of solving a mental maze.
- **EN** At the same time, though, you do mental mazes in a lot of puzzle games, so maybe this is not as special as we would like.
- **EN** One thing to note about the maze design here:
- **EN** it's mostly about whether you can push crystals away from the plateau or not.
- **EN** In a few cases the answer is "Yes, I can get the crystal out if I push it in front of this hole, then drop down into the hole,"
- **EN** but you can only use that trick once, so you have to detect
- **EN** the button that cannot be reached in any other way.

### `audiolog_di_push_down_a_lot_small` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Here, we've simplified the level from 8 buttons to 6, while preserving most of the original ideas.
- **EN** I say 'most' because one idea has been cut from this version,
- **EN** which we mentioned in the previous recording: the part where you *could* reach several different buttons
- **EN** by pushing a crystal in front of a hole, and then dropping into that hole,
- **EN** but you can only do this once, so you choose the right hole to drop into.
- **EN** In this revision, we've streamlined the concepts: "Here are a variety of ways you could reach the buttons,
- **EN** and just one of them involves dropping into a hole".
- **EN** The original version of this level is a bigger, more difficult mental maze.
- **EN** Because this one is not so big, you spend less time maze-solving, and proportionately more time
- **EN** just looking at the individual cases of where the crystals can go.
- **EN** To me, it's a little bit of a matter of taste which version you like more.
- **EN** You might think this one is too easy; but as a designer, keep in mind
- **EN** that it's easy to underestimate the difficulty, because you know the answer already, because you came up with it!
- **EN** In the interest of keeping the pacing quick and avoiding redundant material, we went with the simpler version.

### `audiolog_di_across_the_fence_old` （无官方中译）

- **EN** This is Zach's original design.
- **EN** My experience was that it's pretty hard, and I often didn't have a clear idea of what I was trying to accomplish,
- **EN** until I managed to stumble to the goal; then, even at the end,
- **EN** I couldn't quite tell you the idea of the puzzle,
- **EN** except that it's about going back and forth between the rooms, which as I mentioned, is hard.
- **EN** I'm not saying this to denigrate the puzzle -- I think it's good and interesting,
- **EN** but at the same time, sometimes you'll find yourself faced with this kind of situation,
- **EN** where something just doesn't gel in the Player's mind the way you wish it did.
- **EN** We don't know why Zach made this a Thief level;
- **EN** the activity of Pulling does not have anything obvious to do with the core idea,
- **EN** and it pushes the level toward being bigger, because the Thief cannot drag things to the wall.
- **EN** This pollutes the level a bit, because the Thief's pulling ability is off topic.
- **EN** I've spoken elsewhere about how we try to avoid adding irrelevant objects and ideas to a level.
- **EN** This extends to character abilities.
- **EN** So in a level like this one, where you just need to move things and it doesn't matter how,
- **EN** it's cleaner and simpler to use a character who can only push one thing,
- **EN** because pushing one thing in this game is "the default idea" and introduces the least noise.

### `audiolog_di_across_the_fence_vtrader_m_p` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Here we've shrunk the level substantially, and the Trader is now the main character,
- **EN** which removes the distracting question of whether Pulling matters;
- **EN** now the level uses ingredients from only two worlds,
- **EN** instead of the three we had before, and that's simpler too.

### `audiolog_di_sender`

- **EN** This one is about disappearing a Monster by teleporting it to infinity, in the vertical direction.
  **CN** 这一关的构思，是沿垂直方向把怪物传送到无穷远处，让它消失。
- **EN** Which is a neat idea, but requires some contrivance to set up.
  **CN** 这个构想很巧妙，不过要把它做成关卡，就难免得刻意安排一番。
- **EN** This first attempt is a little hard to read initially,
  **CN** 第一次尝试的版本乍看上去有些难懂，
- **EN** and is somewhat misleading because it places the Goblin
  **CN** 而且还容易误导玩家，问题就出在哥布林的位置上：
- **EN** into a suspicious position, where you can teleport it directly onto the button --
  **CN** 这个位置很容易让人多想，因为你能把它直接传送到按钮上……
- **EN** which is not part of the solution.

### `audiolog_di_sender_simplified`

- **EN** Here's another attempt at this level. Now there's only one character, who can go up and down at will.
  **CN** 这是对本关的另一次尝试。现在只剩一个角色，可以随意上下移动。
- **EN** I didn't want to add an Elevator, so I used a second Gate for you to go up and down,
  **CN** 我不想再加一部升降机，就用了第二道界门，让你能上下移动，
- **EN** which I felt was more on-theme... you raise an object with a Gate to get it to the plateau,
  **CN** 我觉得这也更贴合关卡主题：你先用一道界门把物体抬到高台上，
- **EN** and later you raise a Goblin with the other Gate to make it disappear.
  **CN** 之后，你再用另一道界门把哥布林抬高，让它消失。
- **EN** Ideally I'd have wanted to figure out how to do both of these raisings with one Gate, but I never did. So there are two Gates,
  **CN** 要是能用一道界门完成这两次抬升就好了， 可我始终没想出办法，所以这里用了两道界门，
- **EN** with weird geometry around them, and it's now very hard to read the level --
  **CN** 两道界门周围的地形还奇形怪状，弄得这关非常难看懂……
- **EN** you need to do a bunch of Sokobanning to set up the final trick of the level, but this is very hard to visualize in advance.
  **CN** 你得先做许多推箱子操作，把物体摆到位，最后才能让哥布林消失。 但事先很难想清楚该怎么布置。
- **EN** You might think this is some kind of ideal of tight puzzle design, using objects for several different purposes;
  **CN** 你或许会觉得，让物体承担好几种用途，正是紧凑型谜题所追求的理想设计，
- **EN** for example you use the Gem to push the Goblin around and also to power the socket,
  **CN** 比如，宝石既用来推动哥布林，又用来给插槽供能，
- **EN** but in this case it just fogs up the core idea.
  **CN** 可在这一关里，这么做只会让核心思路更难看清。
- **EN** Compare this to the previous version, which had a vertical Gate
  **CN** 对比一下前一版，那里有一道垂直界门，
- **EN** with no apparent function out in the middle of the floor --
  **CN** 孤零零地立在场地中央，看不出有任何作用……
- **EN** You come into the level, and, obviously you are supposed to do something with that Gate, but what?
  **CN** 你一进入关卡就知道：这道界门显然是要拿来做什么的。可究竟做什么呢？
- **EN** It gave you something very clear to notice and think about. And that's missing from this version.
  **CN** 那一版让你很清楚该注意和琢磨什么，这一版却没有做到。

### `audiolog_di_sender_simplified_v2`

- **EN** This version of the level, which we chose as the final,
  **CN** 这个版本就是我们最终采用的方案，
- **EN** is even more economical than the previous one:
  **CN** 比前一版还要精简：
- **EN** we've gotten rid of the weird confusing geometry and the double vertical Gates.
  **CN** 我们去掉了那些古怪、难懂的地形，也去掉了两道垂直界门。
- **EN** Because raising objects to the upper level is not part of the core idea, we have streamlined that out:
  **CN** 此外，把物体抬到上层并不属于核心思路， 所以我们也把这一步精简掉了：
- **EN** the gem and Beamcaster start at the top, and you just need to move them down.
  **CN** 宝石和光束发射器一开始就在上方，你只需把它们弄下来。
- **EN** It's still a little difficult, and the vertical Gate is slightly obfuscated because you use it to get up to the plateau;
  **CN** 这一版还是有一点难；垂直界门的核心用途也不那么直观， 因为你还会用它登上高台。
- **EN** but because there's only one Gate, and nothing else to do, it's still a focus.
  **CN** 但关卡里只有这一道界门，也没有别的事可做，所以它依然是重点。
- **EN** The way it's slightly hidden feels fairer, and more like a stylistic flourish.
  **CN** 这样稍微藏一藏解题思路，感觉更公平，也更像是在设计上添了点巧思。
- **EN** This version of the level is still perhaps a little harder than it "should" be,
  **CN** 这个版本的难度可能还是比“应有水平”稍高一点，
- **EN** since even after you get the basic idea of what to do, it's easy to get a little confused.
  **CN** 因为即使已经大致知道该怎么做，玩家还是很容易有点犯迷糊。
- **EN** At least for me, the temptation is to try to use both the Beamcaster and the Gem
  **CN** 至少对我而言，很容易忍不住想同时利用光束发射器和宝石，
- **EN** to push the Goblin horizontally in X and Y so it's next to the Gate,
  **CN** 沿X轴和Y轴两个水平方向推动哥布林，把它推到界门旁，
- **EN** and then somehow get the Beamcaster down to the bottom without being killed.
  **CN** 再想办法把光束发射器移到下方，同时保证自己不被杀死。
- **EN** This can't be done; instead, you want to put the Beamcaster on the lower level first.
  **CN** 这个办法行不通；正确做法是先把光束发射器放到下层。
- **EN** We could eliminate this extra confusion by starting the Beamcaster on the bottom (since it'd be impossible to get it onto the plateau),
  **CN** 我们可以让光束发射器从一开始就在下方， 省掉这层多余的困惑（因为那样就不可能再把它弄上高台），
- **EN** but then maybe we are closer to just giving the player the answer, which doesn't feel good to me.
  **CN** 但这么做又太像是直接把答案送给玩家，我不太喜欢这种感觉。
- **EN** All of this is subjective, and you could come to your own conclusion.
  **CN** 不过，这些也都只是我的个人看法，你可以有自己的判断。
- **EN** But I think, because this is a late-game level, maybe some extra difficulty is okay.
  **CN** 但我想，既然这是后期关卡， 稍微多一点难度也许无妨。
- **EN** Look how ugly the previous two versions were, geometrically, compared to this one.
  **CN** 从地形上看，前两个版本和这一版比起来，简直无法直视。
- **EN** Most puzzles can be improved through this kind of iteration.
  **CN** 大多数谜题都能通过这样的迭代来改进。
  *导演注释：switching gears*
- **EN** It's a little humbling to know that your puzzles could always be better,
  **CN** 知道自己的谜题永远还有改进余地，多少会让人不敢自满；
- **EN** and for a game of significant size, it's very very hard
  **CN** 而对一款规模不小的游戏来说，
- **EN** to get anywhere close to the best versions of all the puzzles.
  **CN** 哪怕只是把所有谜题都打磨到接近理想的水平，也非常困难。

### `audiolog_di_worlds_collide_heroes_and_promesst`

- **EN** This level went through a number of revisions, being simplified each time.
  **CN** 这一关经历了多次修改，每次都在简化。
- **EN** Here's the initial version, where on the left side,
  **CN** 这是最初的版本，先看关卡左侧：
- **EN** we have an excuse for the Strength beam to be there -- he needs it to get the Displacer --
  **CN** 力量光束有理由登场……他需要借助光束拿到位移器……
- **EN** whereas the Strength beam is really there so that the Priestess can push things.
  **CN** 可力量光束真正的作用，其实是让女祭司能够推动物体。
- **EN** Ideally, the Priestess pushing the Monsters would be interesting and fun, but here it's kind of a slog
  **CN** 照理说，让女祭司推动怪物应该既有意思又好玩，可这里做起来却很麻烦，
- **EN** because there are multiple solutions, and they are all a little hard to see;
  **CN** 因为解法不止一种，而且每一种都有点难看出来；
- **EN** this makes the blocking crystals feel less intentional, and more random.
  **CN** 这就让那些用来阻挡的水晶显得不像是有意安排的，倒像是随手一摆。
- **EN** Also, the Player will probably wonder if he is supposed to use the Double beam for something besides exiting the level (but he isn't).
  **CN** 此外，你还会怀疑双倍光束除了用来离开关卡， 是否另有用途（其实并没有）。

### `audiolog_di_worlds_collide_heroes_and_promesst_v2`

- **EN** In this version, we've gotten rid of all the crystals, which helps the level feel focused.
  **CN** 这一版删掉了所有水晶，让关卡的重心更明显。
- **EN** We also got rid of the Double beam, and allowed ourselves the slight impurity of placing a Monster Gate on the Beamcaster side.
  **CN** 我们还删掉了双倍光束，并做了一点不那么纯粹的处理： 在光束发射器一侧放置怪物界门。
- **EN** We've also made the Monster elimination less messy and more focused:
  **CN** 我们也把消灭怪物的过程理得更清楚，让核心思路更突出：
- **EN** there are 5 Goblins, so you obviously need to make a group of 3,
  **CN** 这里有五只哥布林，显然得让其中三只组成一组，
- **EN** but this can't be done without moving one away from the wall using the Displacer,
  **CN** 但这样的话就必须用位移器把其中一只从墙边移开，否则就无法解题；
- **EN** which is a second way the two sides can affect each other.
  **CN** 这样一来，关卡两侧又多了一层联动。

### `audiolog_di_worlds_collide_heroes_and_promesst_v3`

- **EN** We continued to revise.
  **CN** 我们继续修改。
- **EN** Here, we got rid of the Rocks on the Beamcaster side,
  **CN** 这一版删掉了光束发射器一侧的石头，
- **EN** since they served no interesting purpose, except that you would displace one of them through the wall between the worlds --
  **CN** 因为除了把其中一块穿过世界之间的墙壁移到另一侧， 它们的用法都没什么意思……
- **EN** for that purpose, we have placed a second Gem instead.
  **CN** 为了保留这个用途，我们改放了第二颗宝石。
- **EN** The fact that there are two Gems and only one socket
  **CN** 关卡里有两颗宝石，却只有一个插槽，
- **EN** should immediately raise questions about what the second Gem is for.
  **CN** 应该会让玩家立刻产生疑问：第二颗宝石是做什么的？
- **EN** Instead of 5 monsters, we cut it down to 3,
  **CN** 怪物则从五只减到了三只，
- **EN** so the challenge is clearly to put all 3 together.
  **CN** 目标也很明确：把三只全部凑到一起。
- **EN** The Displacing is even more interesting now, since you have to use the Priestess herself
  **CN** 位移机制现在更加有趣，因为你必须把女祭司本人也算作额外的质量，
- **EN** as additional mass to move the Goblin through the wall.
  **CN** 才能把哥布林位移到墙的另一边。
- **EN** Unfortunately, though, there's a problem with this little Goblin room.
  **CN** 但很遗憾，这个小小的哥布林房间有个问题。
- **EN** The intended solution is to displace him through the wall,
  **CN** 预期解法是把哥布林位移到墙的另一边，
- **EN** (after having moved the other Goblins north out of the way),
  **CN** （得先把其他哥布林向北移开，腾出地方。）
- **EN** then south, east 1 square, and north, to make a 3-wide Goblin group.
  **CN** 再依次向南、向东一格、再向北移动，让三只哥布林横向排成一组。
- **EN** But there's another solution, where you just push the two outer Goblins south by a square,
  **CN** 但还有另一种解法：只需把外侧的两只哥布林向南推一格，
- **EN** then zap the third guy through the wall to make a 3-wide group.
  **CN** 再把第三只传送到墙的另一边，凑成横向并排的三只。
- **EN** This isn't the worst exploit, since it's still the same basic idea,
  **CN** 这个取巧方式倒不算太糟，因为核心思路仍然相同，
- **EN** but we wanted this level to require more use of the Strength Beam. So we fixed that in the next version...
  **CN** 但我们希望玩家能更多地运用力量光束。 所以下一版修正了这个问题……

### `audiolog_di_worlds_collide_heroes_and_promesst_v4`

- **EN** Here we moved the location of the Power Socket, so that the Priestess can't push the two Goblins south,
  **CN** 这里我们移动了能量插槽的位置， 使得女祭司无法把两只哥布林向南推，
- **EN** fixing the exploit from the previous version.
  **CN** 从而堵上了上一版的取巧解法。
- **EN** We also made the third Goblin's area less of an enclosed room,
  **CN** 我们还让第三只哥布林所在的区域不再那么像一个封闭房间，
- **EN** to avoid suggesting moves that are irrelevant, like displacing objects through
  **CN** 以免让玩家想到与解谜无关的操作，
- **EN** the longer stretch of wall that existed in that version of the level.
  **CN** 比如让物体穿过上一版里那段较长的墙壁。
- **EN** Perhaps this version of the level feels a couple rows too tall on the Heroes side,
  **CN** 这一版的英雄世界一侧纵向看着有些空，像是多留了两行；
- **EN** but the Beamcaster side needs that space: you need two empty rows on the south to displace the Beamcaster back north.
  **CN** 但光束发射器那边需要这些空间： 南边必须空出两行，才能把发射器移回北边。

  **CN** 这一关展示了一种出人意料又颇为有趣的互动：

  **CN** 嗯，通常得理解才行。前提是我们的设计做得够好。
- **EN** 
  **CN** ……我们就是这样前往遥远的空间，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 或是完全不相连的空间……比如你在这座仓库中看到的这些世界。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为宇宙遵循全息理论运行…… 我们最早通过研究黑洞窥见了这一点……
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我猜你们那里也有黑洞，只是还没发现。
  *导演注释：As Director looks at him for confirmation*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 总之，一片空间里有什么，完全取决于边界上记录的信息。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 那些信息具体是什么很难解释，因为它们全都混在了一起。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 如果你想去某个地方，并且能复制那里边界上的条件，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 把出发地的边界条件调整得与目的地完全一致，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 就可以让两片空间连同其中的一切互换位置。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所需能量与边界的表面积成正比，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 不过，置换小块空间所需的能量， 跟我们能调动的能量相比，其实不算多。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 说来有趣，这项研究最初是为了弄清历史上那些天降鱼群的事件——
- **EN** 
  **CN** 鱼就那么从天上落下来，这种自然现象已经得到反复证实，
  *导演注释：an aside; here he shifts tone into telling a story, for the rest of the piece.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 其中也有可靠观察者的记录，却始终没有合理解释。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 于是，有人开始认真琢磨这些鱼怎么会从天而降， 并提出了“边界置换”的设想，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 他研究了很多年，终于让这项技术能作用于极小的东西， 再逐步扩大到宏观空间，最终达到人体大小。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 于是，我们掌握了空间传送。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可他最后意识到，边界置换其实解释不了天降鱼群。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 两边的边界差异实在太大，根本无法匹配。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以这至今仍是一个未解之谜，短期内也不会有答案了，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为我们熟悉的地球早已不复存在……
- **EN** 
  **CN** 完整的来龙去脉很复杂，不过这是其中一部分。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 如果你能接受先从极小的一块空间开始，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你可以猜测目标区域的边界可能是什么样子，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 不断试下去，直到试出点结果。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 也许你会失败很多次。但假设最终，你成功完成了一次置换。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这道边界围出的空间极小，只容得下几个粒子，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但这几个粒子已经能充当传感器，先为你探得一些信息。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 有了这些信息，就可以把边界扩大一点，再试着置换。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 如果结果不理想，就加入一点随机变化，换个条件再试。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 稍大一点的边界置换成功后，就送去稍大一点的传感器。 你会得到稍多一点的信息。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 不断重复，直到被置换的空间能装下你自己。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 真正起作用的边界条件很难解释清楚。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 众所周知，量子态无法克隆。 但在这里不必克隆，因为很多东西会相互抵消。
  *导演注释：(After a brief pause for thought)*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 没错。总之，真正重要的不是边界上某一个点的条件， 甚至也不是所有点的条件。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为我刚说了，它们全都混在一起。 这就是全息理论。真正重要的是整体边界值，
  *导演注释："Sure." As in, it's not really going to be that simple.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 正因如此，我们才能做到一些原本不可能的事。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 简直像是有人特意这样设计的。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 请教导我，因祢全知； 祢自太初已然存在，以巍巍双翼展覆世间，
  *导演注释：TRANSLATOR NOTE: This is exactly the same text as in paradise_lost_invocation_2, but with a small amount added at the end. The translation should be the same.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 如鸽孵卵般覆于浩瀚深渊，使其孕育万物：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我心中幽暗处，求祢照明；卑下处，求祢高举；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 使我得以论述这宏大的主题，申明永恒的天意， 向世人昭示神灵之道的公义。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 世世无穷。愿一切遵照祢的旨意。阿门。

### `audiolog_u_meeting` （无官方中译）

- **EN** 6 or so, on my side.
- **EN** I'm still out there ... investigating. You'll have to keep running things for now.
- **EN** Or appoint a successor if you're tired.
- **EN** It's going *somewhere*. Well or poorly, too soon to say.
- **EN** This guy you want to promote to Sokoban.
  *导演注释：Asking why he's here.*
- **EN** Oh, I trust you. You've done it before, and that last one seems to have acclimated pretty well, strange as he is.
- **EN** He's paying his dues. But regarding your *new* outlander Sokoban,
  *导演注释：Referring to Magistrate Reynard*
- **EN** I want to have some conversations with him. For reasons relating to my inquiry.
- **EN** The topics will wander. You can nose in, if you want.
- **EN** But first, let's catch up...!
  *导演注释："You might get very bored."*

### `audiolog_u_computation` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Do you recognize this?
- **EN** It's one of the simplest examples of running rules on repeated state --
  *导演注释：Leamington's world doesn't have computers; Director is showing him an interactive Mandelbrot set running on a handheld hypercomputer.*
- **EN** where the starting state itself is just always zero.
- **EN** Square it, add the two-dimensional constant for that position in the plane, and that's the new number, and repeat.
- **EN** See how long your number survives, and color accordingly. And, you see ...
  *导演注释：Todo: This used to read "Multiply the number by itself,", I changed to match audio - Charles*
- **EN** Glad you understand. I'll give you this to play with. The important thing to internalize --
- **EN** and absorb over and over in an attempt to understand ever-more-deeply --
- **EN** is that the math is so simple, obviously we don't put the complexity in there, ourselves.
- **EN** That complexity and structure is just *already there* somehow. It's fundamental.
- **EN** How about this one, have you seen it?
- **EN** I'll leave you to play with it. This one is called "Life",
- **EN** named long ago, conceived as an analogy for biological cells reproducing and dying.
- **EN** It's another example of applying simple rules, repeatedly, to initial world state.
- **EN** This time the rules are exactly the same at every point in the space.
- **EN** Experiment; see what you can figure out.

### `audiolog_u_boundary_matching_1` （无官方中译）

- **EN** ... and that's how we travel to spaces that are far,
- **EN** or completely disconnected -- like the worlds you see in this Warehouse.
- **EN** Because the universe acts holographically -- as we first gleaned from the study of black holes --
- **EN** I suspect you have them too, just haven't found them yet.
  *导演注释：As Director looks at him for confirmation*
- **EN** Anyway, the upshot is, everything inside a volume of space is determined by some information at the boundary.
- **EN** The exact nature of that information is hard to explain, as it's all mixed up together.
- **EN** But, if there's someplace you want to go, and you're able to duplicate the conditions on its boundary,
- **EN** by changing the boundary of the place you're starting from, to match precisely --
- **EN** then you can swap the two boundaries and all contents therein.
- **EN** The *amount* of energy is proportional to the surface area,
- **EN** but it's not really so much for smaller spaces. Not compared to what we have available, anyway.
  *导演注释：Todo: Script used to read "The *amount* of energy required is proportional to the surface area of the boundary" - Charles*
- **EN** Funny how this line of inquiry started; they were trying to resolve all these historical cases where it had been raining fish --
- **EN** just fish falling from the sky, a natural phenomenon confirmed repeatedly,
  *导演注释：an aside; here he shifts tone into telling a story, for the rest of the piece.*
- **EN** including by reliable observers. No plausible explanation.
- **EN** So a guy is thinking hard about how this could happen. He comes up with this boundary-swapping idea,
- **EN** and, after years, gets it to work on tiny tiny things. He builds it up to handle macroscopic and eventually human-sized spaces.
- **EN** And now we can travel.
- **EN** But in the end he realized that boundary swapping couldn't explain the fish.
- **EN** The boundary conditions would just have been too different; unmatchable.
- **EN** So that's a mystery we never solved, and won't any time soon,
- **EN** since the Earth as we knew it is now long gone...

### `audiolog_u_boundary_matching_2` （无官方中译）

- **EN** The full story is complicated, but that's part of it.
- **EN** If you are content with a volume that's extremely small,
- **EN** you can take a guess what a boundary might look like inside the destination you want,
- **EN** and keep trying til you get something.
- **EN** Maybe you fail quite a number of times, but suppose, eventually, you successfully get a swap.
- **EN** Your boundary is so small, it can fit just a few particles,
- **EN** but they act as an initial sensor. That sensor gives you some information.
- **EN** With that information, you try a slightly bigger boundary.
- **EN** If you don't quite get it right, try perturbing things a little, at random.
- **EN** When you succeed with the slightly bigger boundary, send a slightly bigger sensor. You get slightly more information.
- **EN** Keep repeating this until you fit inside the swapped space yourself.
- **EN** It's complicated to describe the boundary conditions that actually matter.
- **EN** Famously, you can't clone a quantum state, but here you don't need to, because a bunch of stuff cancels out.
  *导演注释：(After a brief pause for thought)*
- **EN** Sure. Anyway, what's important is not the boundary conditions at any one point, or even all the points.
- **EN** Because, again, it's all mixed up. Holography. What matters is the aggregate value,
  *导演注释："Sure." As in, it's not really going to be that simple.*
- **EN** and this lets us get away with some things that would be impossible otherwise.
- **EN** One might even think it was designed this way.

### `coda_scene_assembled`

- **EN** I am your ardent disciple, heir to your charge...
  **CN** 我是您虔诚的门徒，继承了您的使命……
- **EN** If it be granted me to survive this day, I will guard these your relics
  **CN** 若我有幸活过今日，我定会守护您的这些圣物，
  *导演注释：This is the same as 'coda' and does not have to be translated or acted separately. It is the same text and scene. This is more like a fake entry that has one speaker the whole time, so that we can play it in the game and not get confused by trying to do different files for different actors, like everything else works. This file needs to exist for every voice language in the game, but you would pre-build it into one file with the acting and sound effects in place. See the English version for any questions. Here we lie and say the speaker is 'director' the whole time.*
- **EN** for which we have searched *so long*... let them not be lost,
  **CN** 这些我们寻觅了那么久的圣物……愿它们不再遗失，
- **EN** let them be set forth for all to see, a beacon;
  **CN** 愿它们展现在世人面前，成为指引方向的明灯，
- **EN** and with your image in our people's hearts, stoking hope and inspiration, I will lead them forth into renewal.
  **CN** 让您的形象留在百姓心中，燃起希望、激励人心， 我将引领他们重获新生。
- **EN** But if I am never to leave this place, if the road leads only into the Maw, let me go alone;
  **CN** 但若我注定无法离开此地； 若前路只会通向那深渊巨口，就让我独自前行吧；
  *导演注释：This sounds positive, but it's more like a positive image she *used* to hold that she has given up on today since the situation seems impossible.*
- **EN** let my men be not buried with me. Protect them, whom you must reward for their faithfulness,
  **CN** 别让我的部下与我一同入土。 请保护他们，他们的忠诚应当得到您的回报，
- **EN** and bring them safely through this day, that they may return to their chidren and their wives.
  **CN** 让他们平安度过今日，回到孩子与妻子身边。
- **EN** They will remember me alongside you, and our legend may yet shine into the future and do much good.
  **CN** 他们会把我和您一同铭记， 我们的传奇故事或许仍能照亮未来，造福世人。
- **EN** But I --
  **CN** 可我……
  *导演注释：Actually some hope here when talking about the future that she is no longer in.*
- **EN** My Queen! Urgently!
  **CN** 女王陛下！情况紧急！
- **EN** What??? What is it???
  **CN** 什么？！出什么事了？！
- **EN** ...
  **CN** ……
  *导演注释：Very irritated as he's broken her out of the trance, and also, she doesn't want to be seen in this emotional state*
- **EN** Seize that traitor!
  **CN** 拿下那个叛徒！
  *导演注释：we hear an arrow streaking in, and a pop as the Queen disappears, and the arrow thuds into the tree right behind where she was kneeling*
- **EN** Wha...
  **CN** 什么……
  *导演注释：turning to see the source of the shot; shouting to three men nearby*
- **EN** Is this... the afterlife?
  **CN** 这里……是死后的世界吗？
  *导演注释：Turning back to where the Queen was...*
- **EN** What dark enchantment... Queen, my Queen, where are you? Have they...
  **CN** 这是什么邪术……陛下！ 女王陛下，您在哪儿？难道他们……
  *导演注释：(faintly echoing; she is no longer here)*
- **EN** How long has it been?
  **CN** 过了多久？
  *导演注释：He can't believe his eyes; she has disappeared. He's looking around the nearby rocks and bushes.... surely she's here somewhere? Did that just-shot arrow somehow make her magically disappear? "What dark enchantment" is to self; the part after is calling out.*
- **EN** Get behind!
  **CN** 躲到后面去！
  *导演注释：she reappears, somewhat disorienting, no trace of being distraught as she was just a moment ago.*
- **EN** Not 10 seconds! You were ... just gone!
  **CN** 还不到十秒！您刚才……突然就不见了！
- **EN** Well then, you may finish your report.
  **CN** 行，你可以接着汇报了。
  *导演注释：(he rushes with her to a spot behind a big fallen tree trunk)*
- **EN** It is an honor to fight for you, my Queen; stick close, we've no more time.
  **CN** 能为您而战，是我的荣幸，陛下。跟紧我，没时间了。
- **EN** Inner Guard, rally! Hold nothing back, as we are surrounded!
  **CN** 近卫军，集结！我们被包围了，全力迎敌！
- **EN** But *they* are also surrounded!
  **CN** 可他们也被包围了！
  *导演注释：(at 'Inner Guard' he is shouting to everyone within 100 feet)*
- **EN** ...Who...?
  **CN** ……谁……？
  *导演注释：(loudly, brashly, from nowhere)*
- **EN** You!
  **CN** 你！
- **EN** It is time. North!
  **CN** 时机已到。北边！
  *导演注释：(simultaneously w/ Jackays above; she recognizes his voice, is confused by how she could be hearing that voice in this place and time, but also, she is starting to understand, and realizes this is her salvation)*
- **EN** Aiah! Go to *her*, over there!
  **CN** 艾娅！去她那儿，就在那边！
- **EN** East!
  **CN** 东边！
  *导演注释：Aiah is the Priestess' name (nobody knows this but Thief)*
- **EN** In place!
  **CN** 就位！
- **EN** West!
  **CN** 西边！
  *导演注释：There's a push and a mirror teleport sound, and the sound of Priestess protecting the Queen*
- **EN** On it!
  **CN** 交给我！
- **EN** Hail, not-so-good fellows!
  **CN** 各位不怎么正派的朋友，你们好啊！
  *导演注释：We hear the sound of a gem placement and the endpoint beam powering up*
- **EN** Aaaaaand South!
  **CN** 接下来……南边！
  *导演注释：Bard has teleported via the endpoint beam. (playing her instrument)*
- **EN** Engaging!
  **CN** 开始行动！
- **EN** I believe I've found my new hobby. Now, who's next?...
  **CN** 看来我找到新爱好了。那么，接下来轮到谁了？……
  *导演注释：Endpoint off mirror drowns some guys. After she says "Engaging": We hear Sailor push a crystal skipping stone that skips quickly, then it gets mirrored and cloned, and two of these come to rest on land. We hear a volley of arrows thudding into one of these.*

### `psalm_139_part_2`

- **EN** 
  **CN** 你能用鱼钩钓上利维坦吗？能用垂下的绳索缚住它的舌头吗？
- **EN** 
  **CN** 它岂会向你连连恳求，向你说温柔的话语？
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你把手按在它身上，只须想想那场争战，便不敢再来。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 它的肢体、它的力量，以及匀称的形体，我都不能缄默不言。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 它以自己的鳞甲为傲。其鳞甲片片紧合，如同密闭的封印。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 鳞甲彼此相连，连风也透不进去。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 它打喷嚏便发出光来，双眼犹如晨曦。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 它的气息可点燃煤炭，火焰从它口中喷出。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 它的心坚如石，硬如磨。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 它一挺身，勇士也为之惊惧，在它的冲击下仓皇退避。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 它视铁如干草，视铜如朽木。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 长枪在面前飕飕作响，它也只是嗤笑。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 世间没有任何生灵能与它相比；它生来便无所畏惧。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 它俯视一切高傲的生灵，是所有桀骜者的王。

### `audiolog_u_job_41_fragments` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Canst thou draw out leviathan with an hook? or his tongue with a cord which thou lettest down?
- **EN** Will he make many supplications unto thee? Will he speak soft words unto thee?
- **EN** Lay thine hand upon him, remember the battle, do no more.
- **EN** I will not conceal his parts, nor his power, nor his comely proportion.
- **EN** His scales are his pride, shut up together as with a close seal.
- **EN** One is so near to another, that no air can come between them.
- **EN** By his neesings a light doth shine, and his eyes are like the eyelids of the morning.
- **EN** His breath kindleth coals, and a flame goeth out of his mouth.

- **EN**

  **CN** 这是乔纳为最早的一批盗贼关卡所做的原始设计。

- **EN**

- **EN**

  **CN** 只是用来暗示网格还在往外延伸。

- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为这是前期训练关，我想采用游戏中经常出现的形状，
- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以这一版改成了一连串由走廊相连的房间。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们保留了按钮所造成的有趣局面，
- **EN**

  **CN** 再把水晶放到远处。走到水晶那儿很容易，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 难的是把它一路拖回去，穿过这些房间。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这些房间要绕的路多得有点离谱，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 光是把水晶送到想要的位置，你就得兜好几个圈。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这个设计是我在拿盗贼的移动规律找乐子；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 不过也可以说，关卡地图本身就是一幅盗贼移动规律的示意图，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 房间边界恰好圈出了最有意思的活动范围。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一版里，我们放弃了初版围绕柱子和转角展开的构思。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 新的设计重点落到了墙上：盗贼永远无法把一个物体一路拉到墙边，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这也让局面变得有些滑稽。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这是扎克的初版。关卡创意很巧妙，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可几何布局完全没法让玩家看清解题思路。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 通往目标岛的路线十分迂回，很难在脑中理清……
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而你得先对如何抵达终点有个明确思路，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 才能开始琢磨关卡的核心思路：在某个位置使用镜子，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 一开始它能帮上忙，之后却又会挡住你。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以，关卡布局不够清楚，玩家一开始就很难看懂， 玩起来也容易烦躁。更糟的是：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 场上有三面镜子和一块水晶，玩家很自然就会想把水晶复制一大堆， 可那在这里其实根本没用。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 事实上，关卡甚至像是在暗示你复制水晶，因为其他关卡确实有这种玩法。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这样一来，不光是正确路线难以看清，关卡还在主动误导玩家； 就算不至于误导，也会严重分散注意力。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以，玩家在这里大半时间都摸不着头脑，也就不奇怪了。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这是帕特里克的改版。 核心思路相近（把镜子下方的水晶传送出去），

  **CN** 但这么做的理由完全不同；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而且这里要做两次，感觉更完整，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 也能让你把这个思路记得更牢。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 最重要的是，与初版不同，你很容易就能看清该走哪条路线，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这样玩家就不用再为路线问题犯迷糊了。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 玩家很清楚，自己得想办法穿过那些界门。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一关的构想，是利用镜子改变龙的朝向；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 既然有东南西北四个方向，让玩家朝这四个方向喷火烧掉哥布林， 也就顺理成章。

  **CN** 因为这关设计的那会儿，我们还觉得关卡总得够难，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 还得让玩家小心安排推箱子的每一步，因此困住玩家的那块地方非常小，

  **CN** 想解开谜题，就得小心地调整位置。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 后来，我们逐渐建立起关于“什么样的难度才值得保留、 能让玩家有所收获”的审美判断，

  **CN** 我就觉得这关太憋屈了。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 会让人很挫败，因为你总是不小心走进龙息区域，害死自己。
- **EN** 
  **CN** （通常是被镜子反射后的龙息，你根本看不到任何预警。）
- **EN** 
  **CN** 在这么小的空间里，几乎躲不开，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以，你相当一部分步数都花在了事先微调位置上，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这样才能把镜子推到想要的位置，又不至于丧命。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 在这一关的后续改版中，你会看到我们扩大了中央区域；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这也是在这里展示本关的主要原因之一，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 看看空间大小会如何影响关卡的可玩性。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这个设计除了空间太挤，至少还有两个严重缺陷。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 第一，很容易用镜子把哥布林传送进中央区域，或传到外围另一边；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以，如果玩家还没理清谜题，只是在尝试各种办法， 很容易就会把局面搞得一团糟，玩起来也没什么意思。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而且这样一来，其实不必朝四个方向都喷火，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为你可以传送哥布林，重复利用其中一些方向，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这就破坏了核心思路：我们要的是四个方向都用上！
- **EN** 
  **CN** 第二，“出人意料的最后一步”：杀死第五只哥布林。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一步相当难想到，也让关卡偏离了重点，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为你费了那么多心思，琢磨的却不是怎么朝四个方向喷火。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 作为收尾的步骤，它的好处是要求玩家想出一个非常具体的办法，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可也显得有些生硬；没错，它确实要求玩家让龙朝向特定方向，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但会让人感觉和前面关卡的解法没什么联系。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为收尾这一步很难想到，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 收尾这一步给玩家留下的印象， 可能比我们想让你记住的核心思路还深。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一版把中央区域从5×5格扩大到7×7格。 谜题主体因此容易了些，也许有些过于容易。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 在我看来，流程过于直白了。作为玩家，我只会想：
- **EN** 
  **CN** “好吧，我照你说的步骤做完了，所以就过关了。那又怎样？”

  **CN** 我们要尽量避免这种感受，因为这是游戏，不是家庭作业。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为外墙是实心的，用镜子传送哥布林没那么容易， 所以你再也没法把场面搞得一团糟了；

  **CN** 但你可以先清空东侧或南侧的一座岛，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 再把另一只哥布林通过镜子传送到那座岛上，

  **CN** 所以根本问题仍然存在……
- **EN** 
  **CN** 不过情况已经有所改善，因为你没法把哥布林传到中央随便哪个位置。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一版也有个“出人意料的最后一步”：淹死那条龙；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 不过，比起先前的版本，这一步很容易想明白；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以，这一步更像是给关卡画上一个圆满的句号， 而不是一道喧宾夺主的难题。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这里又是一片7×7格的区域，不过这次中间多了一堵墙。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这堵墙占去了一点多余的空间，但也给你提供了另一种办法，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 让你能在需要时穿过龙息区域。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这样一来，谜题不再那么侧重怎样穿过龙息，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而更侧重怎样调整龙的位置和朝向。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你不用为了应付空间太挤的问题，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而做上一大堆多余的推箱操作。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 中央立柱也有助于解决哥布林传送的问题。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 东侧和南侧的哥布林都被放在较靠后的位置，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 因此无法把它们传送进中央区域；而要把其中一只传到另一只的位置，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 要像上一版那样做到这一点，你就得把镜子放到中央立柱所在的位置。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而这当然是办不到的。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以到了这一版，你终于得朝四个方向都喷火了。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你大可以说，这里“杀死最后一只怪物” 这一出人意料的步骤有些跑题，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但你得先把龙以正确的朝向传送到正确的位置，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 然后再逃出笼子，这部分倒挺有意思的。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而且我们把难度调得比原版更低。

### `audiolog_di_height_chooser`

- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一关的核心思路，是利用一束端点光束和一面魔镜， 搭出这样一个局面：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 让你可以选择想升到多高，并传送到那个高度，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 再由光束把你托在空中，好让你踏上平台。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可与此同时，又夹杂了不少别的操作： 你得填上紫色光束远端的空位；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 睡莲还只能用一次，用来抵达对岸；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 还能用魔镜召回飞石，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 甚至在飞石弹跳途中改变它的移动方向；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 同时还会让玩家疑惑：睡莲是不是该使用多次，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 是不是该让飞石沉下去形成地面，等等。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这关的核心设计本来就复杂，不容易看懂， 再加上这么多让人拿不准的地方，玩家就更摸不着头脑了。

### `audiolog_di_height_chooser_v0`

- **EN** 
  **CN** 这就是我们最终采用的版本。 基本构思相同，但我们把它简化了：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 水域、飞石和睡莲这几项机制都删掉了。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你还是得摆好水晶来挡住光束，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但具体做法更有意思，也真正紧扣本关主题。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们把紫色光束放到关卡远端，而不是上方，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这样你就更容易回到那一侧，调整它的位置， 也能少做些烦琐的推箱子操作。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一版更简单，却也更好， 因为玩家的注意力会更多落在核心思路上。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一关被收录进来，就是为了展示简化关卡能带来哪些改进。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 初版的设计中，有些解题步骤是必须完成的： 比如，你得填上紫色光束远端的空位才能浮起来。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但我们设计这些解题步骤的方式，却把玩家弄得一头雾水。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 一般来说，每当你设计一个谜题， 而它不如你希望的那样干净利落时，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你可以逐一审视每个解题步骤，问问自己： 你是否真的有必要以那种特定的方式去完成，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 还是可以用更干净利落的方式来完成。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这里的思路是先摆好这些石头，这样等你跳下去之后，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 就能把它们推到按钮上……
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但按钮的位置很刁钻，高台的形状也很特别，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 要看出怎样才能同时压住所有按钮，并不容易。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一关明确分成两个部分：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 准备阶段，以及把物体推上按钮的阶段。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以重点在于预先规划：一旦跳下去，就没法再调整水晶的位置了。

  **CN** 所以，这一关就像一座大型思维迷宫，你得仔细观察高台的形状，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 琢磨能利用它做些什么。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我很喜欢这一关；它会让玩家清楚意识到，自己正在破解一座思维迷宫。
- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 它主要考验的是，你能不能把水晶从高台边推开。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 有几处的答案是： “能，只要先把水晶推到这个洞口前，再自己跳进洞里。”

  **CN** 但这个技巧只能用一次，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以你得找出那个不用这种办法就绝对碰不到的按钮。
- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 也就是上一段录音里提到的那一点： 有好几个不同的按钮，你原本都能够到，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 做法是把水晶推到洞口前，再跳进那个洞里，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但这个办法只能用一次，所以必须挑对那个洞跳。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 在这一版里，我们把思路简化了：通往按钮的办法有好几种，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但只有一种需要跳进洞里。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 初版关卡是一座更大、更难的思维迷宫。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一版没那么大，你花在破解迷宫上的时间更少，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 相应会花更多时间逐一观察水晶能移到哪里。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 对我来说，更喜欢哪一版，多少取决于个人喜好。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你也许会觉得这关太简单。不过，作为设计师，你得记住一点：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你很容易低估难度，因为你早就知道答案，毕竟答案就是你自己想出来的！
- **EN** 
  **CN** 为了保持明快的节奏，也避免内容重复，我们最终采用了更简单的版本。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这是扎克的初版设计。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我玩下来觉得这一关相当难，也常常不清楚自己到底要做什么，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 直到跌跌撞撞地摸到终点；可即便已经通关，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我还是说不太清楚这道谜题的核心思路是什么，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 只知道要在几个房间之间来回跑，而且很难。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我这么说不是在贬低这道谜题……相反，我觉得它不错，也很有趣，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但有时候，你的确会遇到这样的情况：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 玩家就是没能按你期望的那样，理清其中的思路。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们不知道扎克为什么把它做成盗贼关。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 拉动物体这件事，跟核心思路并没有什么明显的关系，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 反而迫使关卡变得更大，因为盗贼没法把物体一直拖到墙边。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这样反而会干扰关卡的核心思路，因为盗贼拉动物体的能力与主题无关。
- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 角色能力也不例外。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以，像这种只需要移动物体、并不在乎怎么移动的关卡，
- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为在本作中，推动单个物体是“默认机制”，带来的干扰最少。
- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 于是“拉到底重不重要”这个问题便不会再分散注意力；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 现在关卡只用到两个世界的元素，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而不像之前那样用到三个世界，这样也更简单。
