# 代理总监 The Acting Director

> Director 外出期间代掌沉星会；audiolog_sky_* 创教史独白的主要讲述者；自述从 Sokoban 升至 Implementor 再到代理总监。

**统计**：台词块 951 · 英文词数 6555 · 涉及段落 40 · 官方中译覆盖 508/951

---

  *导演注释：a short pause to segue to the next stage of the discussion*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 摘自我们的一部宗教典籍——我确实喜欢这段诗。
  *导演注释："a selection of verse" becase it's not really a poem, it's a condensed version of Job 41 with many omissions in between.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但那些较晚加入的成员（虽说“较晚加入”，他们其实都比我资深） 却完全不搞这一套。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你走进他们的设施，听到的无非是基本说明、 几点帮助，然后就开工。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 也许他们觉得自己的地位还不够高， 不配这样张扬，怕显得僭越。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我想，他们也怕自己会难堪。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 嗯。那时我连构建者都还不是，可我这人脾气倔…… 偏要跟那些创始人一样。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我一直在练习，直到将这篇祷文念得滚瓜烂熟， 再把它放在恰到好处的位置。
  *导演注释：after a pause*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我做的这一切发自真心，自己也很满意。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可后来，闲言碎语就来了。 有人说我听起来装腔作势、空洞无物。
  *导演注释：...*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 也许当时的我确实有那么一点矫揉造作。 但凡事总要有个起点。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我还是昂首挺胸，继续保留诵读祷文的习惯。 那段时间，我的仓库也越做越精巧。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 要是这么停下来，岂不说明那些说闲话的人说得对？！
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可日子久了，我终究还是渐渐不再诵读祷文， 大概是觉得已经过了要这么做的年纪。
  *导演注释：...*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 又或者，我终究还是让那些说闲话的人得逞了。
  *导演注释：...*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但今天与你交谈，让我重新燃起了热情。 这么多年来头一回，我要重新录制这段祷文。 如果你愿意听，也愿意看我录制，我会很高兴。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我会把它放在这座仓库的入口处，
  *导演注释：Shifting tone to something more personal*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这是我有能力建成的最后一座仓库，也会是我即将交给你的第一座仓库。
  *导演注释：The "soon pass over to you" she has recently decided, during these conversations; she is breaking the news to him here.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我的祷文节选自一首非常古老的诗。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 借着它，我重新敞开了内心尘封已久的一部分。 你在任期间，请好好照看它。
  *导演注释：She is referring to the Paradise Lost quote at the start of the game.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这不奇怪。不过，你要是带着别的心思来这里， 时间一长，自然会露出来……
- **EN** 
  **CN** 哦，看看谁来了。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这事私下谈。
- **EN** 
  **CN** ……还有些事情必须立刻处理。试想，任何一个疯子， 都能仅凭一时兴起，就让整座城市—— 连同周边一带——陷入毁灭，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而且这种事已经发生过几次，发生得还越来越频繁。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 那些技术当时还在起步阶段，但大家都迫切想把它发展成熟。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 后来，我们终于做到了：创造新的空间，微调其中的法则， 搬进去安家，再把门关上。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 偶尔才把门打开一条小缝，只进行事先精心安排的会面。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 幸好我们逃了出来，那颗星球不久后就毁灭了； 不过当时的人反而惊讶，它竟然撑了那么久。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 想住哪里都行。
  *导演注释：Wanting a picture of what this looks like.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 从整体看，这些空间像钢丝绒一样缠成一团。 就连较熟悉的区域，要画出地图也很难。
  *导演注释：Spreading her hands to indicate the space around them. "wherever" is short for "wherever you like".*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而且，地图能涵盖的范围也有限。这里的空间大多归私人所有。 无论是谁，都对其中的大部分地方不了解。
  *导演注释："spatial steel wool" means, imagine a bunch of tunnel-like structures (the strands of the wool).*

### `audiolog_sky_take_a_tour` （无官方中译）

- **EN** ... your manners, sir! That look could be taken in quite the wrong way.
- **EN** Let not your heart be troubled. I'm your friend.
- **EN** Lift your nose from the grindstone for a bit.
  *导演注释：Guarded and uncertain at her total openness, which would be uncouth where he came from.*
- **EN** Walk with me, and I will tell you about the history of this place:
  *导演注释："Lift your nose from the grindstone" refers to the way he is grinding through the puzzles without much in the way of breaks. She's been watching him, unseen, for a while.*
- **EN** how it came to be, and why things are the way they are.

### `audiolog_sky_lifeboat_societies_1` （无官方中译）

- **EN** It's much easier to break things than to build them.
- **EN** If, God forbid, you perform blind surgery on an organism, not caring what you cut, or how deeply,
- **EN** then of course you're going to kill it. Random interventions are almost never beneficial.
- **EN** So, what happens when, due to the rapid advances of technology,
- **EN** the entire organism of your society undergoes change, after change, after change,
- **EN** each coming as you've only started to feel the last?
- **EN** It's impossible to imagine if you haven't been there.
- **EN** Suddenly we're incredibly rich, but almost every way we employ these riches
- **EN** becomes deleterious to our well-being, mental *and* physical.
- **EN** Imagine the wealthy children of some businessman,
- **EN** never had to work for anything in their lives,
- **EN** they become snotty, presumptuous, useless, bored,
- **EN** believing they are masters of their world with nothing above.
- **EN** Nothing to look forward to, no belief in even the *possibility* of meaning that could guide their actions.
- **EN** Because they think they're too smart to believe in meaning.
- **EN** "Maybe drugs will give me some reprieve," and we have oh *so* many drugs.
- **EN** They set about destroying body, mind, and spirit in plausibly-deniable ways.
- **EN** When that gets boring, well, why not toy with our weapons of incredible power;
- **EN** turn them on anyone who looks at you askance. Maybe you'll feel better.
- **EN** Technology keeps advancing, and soon provocations become unnecessary.
- **EN** Someone was feeling bad today, so he set off a bomb or unleashed a disease
- **EN** just to see a million souls squirm and die. To vent upon them his rage. His rage at being nothing.
- **EN** There were enough such people to wreck our world;
- **EN** too many of our contemporaries are not much better.
  *导演注释：After a pause, snapping out of it:*
- **EN** These were, and are, dangerous times, and I thank God that I've survived inaswell as I have.
- **EN** Though it's not been without incident for me, either.
  *导演注释："inaswell" is a word that came into use in the future; think of it like "insofar".*

### `audiolog_sky_lifeboat_societies_2` （无官方中译）

- **EN** As I said, I thank God. Beliefs can keep you anchored. They have survival value.
- **EN** They prevent the world becoming meaningless in your mind.
  *导演注释："they have survival value" is a reference to a Darwinian framework of thought*
- **EN** And when founding an organization that keeps a population afloat with its laws and mores,
- **EN** beliefs provide enough structure to hold your vessel together, that it be not broken by the waves.
- **EN** For Man in the state of primitive Nature, life is an onslaught of constraints and limitations.
- **EN** You can barely survive, so long as you do this, then that, and that.
  *导演注释：Shifting gears to go back and explain that life used to have a lot of structure, then most of it disappeared.*
- **EN** Perilous as this existence may be for any individual man,
- **EN** the constraints form a container that protects humanity as a whole.
- **EN** But in response to all the peril, we developed technology.
- **EN** In mastering Nature, we removed the constraints. No more container. A turtle without its shell.
- **EN** So you need to replace the shell. You need a boat with enough of a hull to stay above the water.
- **EN** Only then can you think about sailing it somewhere.
- **EN** Various new societies had different ideas how to accomplish this. Our Order is one such.

### `audiolog_sky_lifeboat_societies_3` （无官方中译）

- **EN** We don't require our members to believe anything specific.
- **EN** Many of our founders have beliefs you would call religious, or "spiritual".
- **EN** Some others don't, but they understand
- **EN** that the structure and machinery of the Order were created according to these beliefs.
- **EN** "It's in the water", as they say.
- **EN** (The water that we drink, that is.)
- **EN** Overall, belief is one of the primary reasons our Order has survived.
  *导演注释：Clarifying the idiom for a foreigner, differentiating from the ocean water they see all around:*
- **EN** Ambition helps too.
- **EN** But without a reason to be that transcends human whim,
- **EN** we'd have dissolved like so many other of the Lifeboat societies.
- **EN** Like the one I was born into.
- **EN** Because even if you make a boat out of rules,
  *导演注释：She was born into the Society for the Preservation of Civil Life, a group that sought to recreate some amalgam of English society from a little before the Victorian era up until 1913.*
- **EN** if everyone knows the rules are arbitrary, why *not* break them, if it feels good in the moment?
- **EN** How would you get everyone to observe arbitrary, meaningless, unbacked rules for even a single century?
- **EN** Many societies chose to be less-structured and to rely on the good judgement of their members,
- **EN** but if you want to know how well they worked, good luck finding anyone to ask.
- **EN** So it goes.

### `audiolog_sky_combiners` （无官方中译）

- **EN** The very earliest Warehouses did not combine worlds, as these do. They were collections
- **EN** of individual elements mixed together: a handful of objects, a handful of behaviors.
  *导演注释："Warehouse" refers to a place like the setting of the game -- an artificial space used for creating puzzles out of disparate elements. This is a somewhat-cheeky, somewhat-respectful nod to the term "Sokoban", which is the genre of game this is, named after a 1980s Japanese computer game. "Sokoban" means "Warehouse Keeper" in Japanese, and is also the name of the rank one earns in the Order when one is given responsibility of a Warehouse (as Acting Director gave to Leamington after these conversations took place).*
- **EN** As the Order's abilities grew, they put more things together at a time.
- **EN** Beyond a certain size, the outcomes tended towards inscrutability.
  *导演注释："they" because she hadn't joined the Order yet.*
- **EN** Some further structure was wanted, to help create clear and visible patterns in the Visitor's mind.
- **EN** So they got the idea to borrow multiple elements from a single world at once, and then to combine those worlds.
- **EN** This was around when I showed up.
- **EN** The Warehouses, that brought two worlds together, we came to call 2-combiners.
- **EN** We did a lot of those. They provided for very clear focus.
- **EN** The High Implementors discussed ways of achieving more interconnectedness,
- **EN** but for a long time we couldn't manage it -- attempts at higher degrees of combination would just produce a mess.
- **EN** Over time, though, we got better. Eventually 3-combiners became achievable, then popular, then standard.
- **EN** The leap to 4 happened much later.
- **EN** Each time you increase the number of worlds, the number of invisible connections rises disproportionately;
- **EN** in the limit it's 2 to the nth power, where n is the number of ideas across all the worlds.
- **EN** So it's hard to keep pushing upward.
- **EN** But doing hard things is the reason we are here.

### `audiolog_sky_combiners_2` （无官方中译）

- **EN** By now, we've done a lot of 4-combiners.
- **EN** We topped out at 4, more or less, for a long while.
- **EN** When I oversaw the building of this Warehouse, I was just following the established pattern,
- **EN** but filling it in a bit differently, applying the best of what I knew.
- **EN** The 4-combiner had become a well-worn template, a path too-frequently trodden, perhaps.
- **EN** The very day we broke ground for this Warehouse, Director -- the Director of the Static Brigade --
- **EN** and Lemur too, announced that for the next facility, we would try something new and big.
- **EN** The 250th anniversary of our Order had arrived;
- **EN** and coincidentally, the serial numbers of the Warehouses were about to reach an arcane and auspicious number.
- **EN** There's a bit of an inside joke that few understood except Lemur, Director and some other Highests.
- **EN** They said we should celebrate.

### `audiolog_sky_combiners_3` （无官方中译）

- **EN** So, celebrate. A more-ambitious Warehouse, of a scale heretofore unimagined!
- **EN** What form should it take? We'd been in a rut of 4-combiners for a while, so a 5-combiner was the obvious next step.
- **EN** But Lemur didn't like the idea. "Too small," he said, and also, "poor symmetry!"
- **EN** See, once we had gotten to 4-combiners we liked them a lot better than 3;
- **EN** the 4-way symmetry splays itself nicely across a two-dimensional map,
- **EN** and besides the practicality of that, it also *celebrates* the dimensional axes in a way.
- **EN** Because the mathematics of space are so fundamental to what we do, 4-way symmetry feels good.
- **EN** A 5-combiner doesn't flatten out nicely into that symmetry,
- **EN** but a *6-combiner* would be nice because it expands into the third dimension,
- **EN** so we now position one world in the positive and negative direction along each axis.
- **EN** For these reasons, we did strongly consider a 6-combiner.

### `audiolog_sky_combiners_4` （无官方中译）

- **EN** But Lemur still said "No!" Director agreed. And many of us saw their point.
- **EN** A 6-combiner would be significantly more ambitious than 4, but could plausibly be completed by
- **EN** a mid-sized group of Implementors, say, one or two hundred.
- **EN** So, what if we went bigger than that? What if we declared a project so audacious, it required all hands for any hope of completion?
- **EN** (One of the reasons we had topped out at 4-combiners was the tradition of having one or a few designers authoring the whole Warehouse;
- **EN** which we did feel was very important for a long time. But part of this decree was to change the way we did things.)
- **EN** We were tired of plodding forward, and our membership was dwindling.
- **EN** Let's give people a real challenge, something so ambitious, bordering on insane,
  *导演注释："membership was dwindling" has been a familiar, constant, problem, and is getting worse.*
- **EN** that minds who had gone gray and dull would be reinvigorated even just hearing about such a thing.
- **EN** Teach them to yearn for the vast and endless sea.
- **EN** We didn't want safe. We wanted to break through to the next level.
  *导演注释：This is a paraphrase of a quote attributed Antoine de Saint-Exupéry, author of The Little Prince.*
- **EN** As our Director put it, we wanted to fashion our own small Leviathan, an endeavor at which we might truly suffer failure;
- **EN** to test ourselves as individuals, and to test the Order as a whole.
- **EN** So. Another of the Highest Implementors suggested stepping it up to an 8-combiner, with hierarchical structure.
- **EN** It'd be shaped as two 4-combiners like this Warehouse we're in,
  *导演注释：She is the Highest Implement of whom she is speaking here.*
- **EN** but after exploring one entire 4-combiner, and then the other, 8 worlds in total,
- **EN** you'd reach the bridge where they connect to each other.
- **EN** "I like the way you're thinking," Lemur said --

### `audiolog_sky_tetratetrad` （无官方中译）

- **EN** "I like the way you're thinking," Lemur said --
- **EN** but why stop there? We've found that symmetry does good things for us, so why stop at the partial symmetry of a two-by-four barbell?
  *导演注释：This is an overlap with the end of the prevoius audiolog. We can read continuously through.*
- **EN** "Keep going!" He prompted us to think about *full* symmetry: a 4-combiner of 4-combiners.
- **EN** We would constuct 4 entire Warehouses, each like in size to this one;
- **EN** and, just like the worlds combine in a cross pattern *here*,
- **EN** that same cross pattern would occur again, writ large, and mix the 4 sub-facilities.
- **EN** The magnitude of the leap was absurd, but Lemur was finally happy with the idea.
- **EN** And we all had to admit, it just looked right. So, that's the way we went.
- **EN** It was an incredibly difficult project that truly required all hands.
- **EN** It did reinvigorate the group; many souls we’d not seen in decades re-emerged from obscurity, smiling wide.
- **EN** As for myself, I had only just begun to design this very Warehouse in which we stand.
- **EN** Even so, I and my assistants felt the need to participate. We worked sometimes here, sometimes there.
- **EN** I hadn't seen anyone work so hard since the early days.
- **EN** We were making good progress. Then, we hit a wall.
- **EN** We'd thought we understood holography quite well, but the Holographic Principle still held surprises.

### `audiolog_sky_why_so_big` （无官方中译）

- **EN** For a long time, every Warehouse we built was much smaller than this.
- **EN** As we refined our art, they got bigger.
- **EN** Even so, some Implementors today still like to keep it simple
- **EN** and build places smaller, and more focused, than they *could* do.
- **EN** But I think, if we're here to study our very prolific universe,
- **EN** well, if you stay in the shallows, you won't learn to swim. Not as well. Not under harrowing circumstances.
- **EN** You do have water that permits you to float, yeah? That metaphor makes sense to you?
- **EN** Good. It's not a given... as you've seen, two of these worlds have no water at all.
  *导演注释：Realizing he may not know what swimming is.*
- **EN** Anyway, yes, this Warehouse is bigger than most. And it was pretty hard to make.
- **EN** Even so, we left some things out. It could have been bigger, "should" have been bigger perhaps.
- **EN** But this was the very limit of what I could do after 10 years of work.
- **EN** Had I known it could be my last, I might have kept pushing.

### `audiolog_sky_why_so_big_3` （无官方中译）

- **EN** This Warehouse is one of the more audacious of the 4-combiners.
- **EN** The North and West worlds here are quite bold -- they import a large number of objects and behaviors
- **EN** that serve as combination fuel.
- **EN** In contrast, the worlds in the East and South only bring in a few objects and behaviors each,
- **EN** which is more typical. But with the North and West, I was feeling frisky.
- **EN** Getting together all those elements in the North was very difficult to eventuate... and took lavish expenditure, I must say.

### `audiolog_s_tradition_of_our_people_part_1` （无官方中译）

- **EN** I find them fun to listen to. We used to do a similar thing. You could say it was a tradition.
- **EN** It's not the 'beacon' part, but the way you record a monologue. For us, the practice goes way back;
- **EN** it was established by the primitive arts from which, eventually, our current work evolved.
- **EN** The old thing was a form of entertainment, usually. You would be exploring some territory;
- **EN** you'd come upon a narrated recording left for you by some fictional character.
- **EN** You listen to it, then explore more, find another. Then another.
- **EN** These recordings would tell you about the character, his thoughts, and the setting as a whole.
- **EN** What they used to term "world-building", funny to think. We'd have to call it differently now.
- **EN** When our Order began its own work, their constructions were not too unlike these old entertainments,
  *导演注释：A wry thought because they actually build worlds now.*
- **EN** highly fictionalized, so they used a similar technique. Over time it blossomed into something more direct.
- **EN** In my own works, I appreciated that these recordings, when well-made, helped to focus and direct the Visitors.
- **EN** It's useful to hint to them about what exactly is going on, why all this is here.
- **EN** The recordings are supposed to be interesting,
- **EN** to shed light on what we're doing in a way that's unique and alive. But ...
  *导演注释：a mild sigh*

### `audiolog_s_tradition_of_our_people_part_2` （无官方中译）

- **EN** The recordings are supposed to be interesting, to shed light on what we're doing in a way that's unique and alive.
- **EN** But, it's hard to stay fresh after hundreds of works ...
- **EN** How do you keep finding new ideas that are really worth hearing?
- **EN** It's difficult to avoid falling into a rut of repeatedly saying the same things, using slightly different words each time.
- **EN** That's not merely dull for us; for the Visitor it feels flat and uninspired.
- **EN** The perceptive ones will see, as we do, that it diminishes the work.
- **EN** The mechanism by which we combine these worlds constantly generates fresh material.
- **EN** We'll talk about this further, I'm sure.
  *导演注释：Shifting to look at it from another direction.*
- **EN** But we find the freshness of the material outpaces what we have to *say* about the material. So we generally agreed, better to stop.
- **EN** High Implementors still do make recordings on occasion, when they find something new to say.
- **EN** Today I suppose I've got a bee in my bonnet.
- **EN** This discussion is long enough that I'll split it into pieces, and place them appropriately.
  *导演注释：This is the segue into the next part ... she is saying that she's about to take *this* discussion and distribute it as recordings according to tradition.*
- **EN** There are tactics determining good placement that you should know.
  *导演注释：now talking about what they will do in this moment.*
- **EN** We leave the first part in an easily-accessible place,
  *导演注释：clarifying "appropriately"*
- **EN** to introduce the discussion as the player enters the area;
- **EN** what we're saying now will be the second part, and we'll leave it in a location that must be deliberately earned,
- **EN** but that is plainly visible on first entry.
- **EN** That gives the Visitor a goal to work toward ... if he's interested.

### `audiolog_sky_play_of_form_1` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Our Order was founded from the meeting of two old 'spiritual' traditions, together with a newer one that's more like your own Natural Philosophy.
- **EN** Let's start with the woo-woo, then! These traditions go back, say, three or four thousand years;
  *导演注释：mild snark*
- **EN** and they each shed a different light on a similar fundamental idea:
  *导演注释：poking at him*
- **EN** that the Universe is engaged in a Play of Form; a constant, effortless action, performed out of the sheer fullness of being.
- **EN** A child becomes an adult becomes an old man, a wild storm whips through the trees, this nation goes to war with that nation:
- **EN** all aspects of this play of form, of possibilities playing out.
- **EN** The important idea to take into the rest of this discussion
- **EN** is that the Universe *wants* to explore different forms;
- **EN** it's not a dead and lifeless progression through time, but somehow abundant and surprise-generating.
- **EN** Now, we get to the part that you'll like more: the methods of the understanding of Natural Law.
- **EN** When our civilizations were young, we might have thought the laws were simple:
  *导演注释：Starting the discussion about more-modern physics.*
- **EN** the sun rises at regular intervals, the tides cycle, objects fall downward and break.
- **EN** But as our sciences advanced, we found that we could refine our notions of these laws,
- **EN** making them successively more specific and accurate: the laws of physics, chemistry, astronomy, and so on.

### `audiolog_sky_play_of_form_1a` （无官方中译）

- **EN** ... the laws of physics, chemistry, astronomy, and so on.
- **EN** So, what is Law? When viewed from inside Time,
- **EN** the physical Law consists of a set of rules, not unlike the rules of a game:
  *导演注释：shifting gears*
- **EN** when thing A and thing B interact, and the properties of A and B
- **EN** are in such and such a way, the rulebook tells you the result.
- **EN** Repeat these rules over and over, and that's physics happening, or chemistry, or any other set of laws you can codify.
- **EN** Indeed.
- **EN** Rules operate on the current state of the world, and give us a new state of the world.
- **EN** We would say they map state to state.
  *导演注释："state of the world" here means positions of objects as well as their internal properties, including potential conscious properties.*
- **EN** But rules don't *dryly* map state to state.
- **EN** When the rules are chosen just right, they generate. They create consequences that are bountiful and sometimes deeply surprising.
- **EN** And that brings us back to those spiritual traditions that say that the Universe is exploring form as an aspect of its own joy.
  *导演注释：elaborating*
- **EN** Our tradition is a descendant of those. We carry it on by seeing this Play of Form as the blooming of lawful possibility:
  *导演注释：shifting slightly*
- **EN** the creative explosion that happens as a result of rules operating on state.
- **EN** When a Mirror and a Skipping Stone come together, and the result generates newness and surprise,
- **EN** we observe this as a specific instance of the Universe's ever-unfolding process of creation.
- **EN** We look with wide eyes; we try to submerge ourselves, soaking it up.
- **EN** Somehow the Universe *wants* the Mirror and the Skipping Stone to play together.
- **EN** When we bring these two elements near each other, some kind of potential is released:
- **EN** this big explosion of possibility happens, and generates surprising ways for Mirror and Stone to interact.
- **EN** And we sift through, we find the consequences that are most delightful and beautiful, or just unexpected,
- **EN** and we frame them, to help Visitors like yourself appreciate this unfolding.
- **EN** That's the why of all these puzzles. The puzzles draw frames around specific instances of unfolding.
- **EN** And many instances, well-displayed together, fill out a rich, multidimensional image in our minds.
- **EN** You see the similarities between one behavior and another.
- **EN** You see the differences. You notice unique moments that seem to communicate something special, that defies verbalization.
  *导演注释：Talking about the aforementioned image. From here forward, she is fondly reminiscing on some of her best times... she is speaking from experience.*
- **EN** From a constellation of examples, we build in our awareness
- **EN** a vision of the Play of Form that we'd never achieve otherwise.
- **EN** You don't have to believe them. A decent fraction of the Order does not!
  *导演注释：He's not super into it.*
- **EN** But your job for now, ignoring what anyone believes or doesn't believe,
- **EN** is just to open your eyes *really wide* and just *see* it. Just see the phenomena.
- **EN** Good. We'll speak again, after you've explored a bit further.
  *导演注释：pause; then, with resolve*
- **EN** See you later, Alligator.

### `audiolog_sky_play_of_form_1b` （无官方中译）

- **EN** We play one specific situation, after another, after another. We learn to generalize.
- **EN** Then we go again. Finished that Warehouse, on to another. More puzzles.
  *导演注释：The verbal repetition in these passages is intentional. It is meant to evoke the repetition of training and practice, of doing something many times until you're very good.*
- **EN** We see specifics. We learn how to generalize between them. We absorb, to our current capacity, the Way of Creation.
- **EN** We meditate on what we have seen. Then we go again. Another ten Warehouses, another hundred.
- **EN** We meditate deeply on what we have seen. We absorb, to our now-increased capacity, the Way of Creation.
- **EN** And there's an outer cycle as well. The better we play and absorb,
- **EN** the better-built are the Warehouses we construct, the better the experiences of our Visitors.
- **EN** We have a special meditation wherein we take the knowledge we've internalized and expand it outward,
- **EN** meeting the world with what we know. Seeing the world in a new way.
  *导演注释：As she is speaking to him, she is doing it from this meditative frame.*
- **EN** Being *inside* the world in a new way.
- **EN** In the beginning we perform these meditations seated, eyes closed.
- **EN** Eventually you can do them eyes open, just walking around.
- **EN** Even when speaking with someone.

### `audiolog_sky_10000_things` （无官方中译）

- **EN** There's an ancient book of our people -- it was reassembled from scraps, so never fully reliable --
- **EN** but it describes the universe emerging from a fundamental whole underlying everything we see.
- **EN** First, somehow, this whole, the Dao, gives rise to One.
- **EN** We'll be simplistic for now and think of this as the Dao identifying itself.
- **EN** This is deeply questionable, but we have to start somewhere. Anyway.
- **EN** Then, the book says, One produces two, two produce three, and three produce all the ten thousand things --
- **EN** all that we observe in Creation.
- **EN** The text is mysterious and minimal; but combined with what we've already discussed,
- **EN** you can guess the Order's angle.
- **EN** Rather than just accepting the results of this creative process, can we look at it more closely?
- **EN** Can we create a microscope to help us zoom in
- **EN** and look at the Dao in all its particular details, to understand how this creative process works?
- **EN** Some of us still think the answer is No.
- **EN** We did too, once. And, well, to physical appearances it somewhat did ... but when you look at underlying mechanisms, they are not so unstable.
  *导演注释：He is talking about a big-bang-like situation.*
- **EN** If you want an image, you can think of this Dao business as describing an explosion that created the Universe,
- **EN** but, somewhere more fundamental. Any physical explosion is just a surface echo.

### `audiolog_s_tradition_of_our_people_part_3` （无官方中译）

- **EN** If the recording is short, too short to be a satisfying reward on its own,
- **EN** don't put it in a hard-to-reach place. We mustn't disappoint!
- **EN** Just put it in an easy place, and it will feel like a small gift,
- **EN** because you're acknowledging the Visitor's progress to a new area.

### `audiolog_s_tradition_of_our_people_part_2b` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Regarding your own recordings... normally they would be considered foreign objects
- **EN** and would be automatically removed upon your exit.
- **EN** But today, I'll allow it. They're nice. They help the place feel lived-in.

### `audiolog_sky_invocation` （无官方中译）

- **EN** and eventually, the day came for me to rise in rank, from Sokoban to Implementor. You do this by creating your own Warehouse for the first time.
- **EN** Something small. Not too fancy. Enough to show you know the basics.
- **EN** But, of course, I wanted *mine* to be special.
- **EN** As part of our training, we must play through all the major works of the Founders.
- **EN** First Founder Lemur's Warehouses all began the same way. At the entrance, right before you start, one of these recordings.
  *导演注释：pause to shift the story*
- **EN** But, not a fictionalized recording. Rather, a cryptic reading --
- **EN** from a poem, half of nonsense, about a monster that makes people disappear --
- **EN** Of course recordings can be copied, but each time, Lemur voiced a fresh recording of the same text.
  *导演注释：The Hunting of the Snark*
- **EN** I felt I came to understand, from reading to reading, the subtle secret meanings behind each pause, each lowering of the voice.
- **EN** The man who is still the official Director of the Static Brigade
- **EN** used to read a selection of verse about a giant sea monster,
  *导演注释：a short pause to segue to the next stage of the discussion*
- **EN** from one of our religious texts -- I do like that one.
  *导演注释："a selection of verse" becase it's not really a poem, it's a condensed version of Job 41 with many omissions in between.*
- **EN** But the newer members (I say "newer", though they were all senior to me), the newer members didn't do any of this.
- **EN** You walked into their facilities, it was just, basic instructions and help, and let's go.
- **EN** Perhaps they felt they didn't rank highly enough to put on such a show. That it would be presumptuous.
- **EN** I think also, they were worried about embarrassing themselves.
- **EN** Well. Before I had even reached the rank of Implementor, headstrong me decided I would be ... just like the Founders.
- **EN** I practiced 'til I performed my invocation well, and I placed it in just the perfect spot.
  *导演注释：after a pause*
- **EN** What I had done felt true. I was happy with it.
- **EN** But, people talked. Some said I sounded pretentious and hollow.
  *导演注释：...*
- **EN** And maybe I was, a little. But we all have to start somewhere.
- **EN** Chin up, shoulders set, I continued this practice of invocation for a while, as my Warehouses grew more sophisticated.
- **EN** Can't just stop and show the gossips that they were right!
- **EN** But over time, eventually, I fell out of the practice. I suppose I felt I'd outgrown it.
  *导演注释：...*
- **EN** Or perhaps I'd let the gossips win after all.
  *导演注释：...*
- **EN** Though today, speaking with you, I feel reinspired. For the first time in many, many years, I'm going to re-record my invocation. I'd be pleased if you listen, and watch me do so.
- **EN** I will place it in the entryway to this Warehouse,
  *导演注释：Shifting tone to something more personal*
- **EN** the last I was able to create, and the first I will soon pass over to you.
  *导演注释：The "soon pass over to you" she has recently decided, during these conversations; she is breaking the news to him here.*
- **EN** My prayer is a small excerpt from a very old poem.
- **EN** With it, I have reopened a part of myself I'd kept shut away for a very long time. Take care of it through your tenure.
  *导演注释：She is referring to the Paradise Lost quote at the start of the game.*

### `audiolog_s_magistrate` （无官方中译）

- **EN** That's not surprising. But long-term, if you're here for inauthentic reasons, it'll become obvious...
- **EN** Oh. Look who we have here.
- **EN** We'll discuss in confidence.

### `audiolog_sky_wool` （无官方中译）

- **EN** ... and some things just require immediate attention. Imagine that any single lunatic can unleash destruction, at a whim, across an entire city -- and the surrounding area --
- **EN** and this has already happened a few times, and the trend is accelerating.

### `end1_dimension_0`

- **EN** Thank you, Sokoban.
  **CN** 谢谢你，守库人。
  *导演注释：In all these end1 conversations, Leamington has been with the Order for a couple of years, and has caught some of the habits of modern speech, but not all of them. We still want to recognize him, and he should sound a little like how he did at the start of the game, but, his choice of words is different now.*
- **EN** I see you've performed the reconnection quite well.
  **CN** 看来你把连接恢复得相当好。
  *导演注释：She just hears voices coming from above, coming from seemingly nowhere, and is not sure how to respond, but knows they are live voices, the first she has heard in quite some time.*
- **EN** Our Visitor hasn't been waiting but a handful of seconds.
  **CN** 我们的访客只等了短短几秒。
- **EN** It's been a while since we last spoke.
  **CN** 我们有一阵子没说话了。
- **EN** You must be working hard.
  **CN** 那你这段时间一定没闲着。
  *导演注释："Proper Time" is a concept from Einsteinian Relativity, but has further meaning when spaces can be causally isolated. It means the amount of time experienced from the viewpoint of someone in that place, as opposed to another place (which might have totally different time).*
- **EN** Don't I know it.
  **CN** 这我可太清楚了。
- **EN** Sokoban, busy as you've been, surely you were informed of the new rule
  **CN** 守库人，你近来虽然忙得不可开交， 但肯定也听说了那条新规：
  *导演注释：(Re-asking the question in a more formal, stateful way, with an edge of demand)*
- **EN** that we mustn't speak directly with any Visitor of insufficient rank,
  **CN** 我们不得直接与任何级别不足的访客交谈，
- **EN** regardless of any upset this may cause.
  **CN** 无论这会让对方多么不快。
- **EN** I did enjoy our conversations, but they caused a kerfuffle.
  **CN** 我确实很享受我们的谈话， 但也正是我们之间的谈话惹出了不小的风波。
- **EN** None needed. It was supposed to have been the rule already.
  **CN** 不用道歉。这规矩本来早该定下来的。
- **EN** As the seniormost present officer of the Order of the Sinking Star,
  **CN** 作为在场级别最高的沉星之序官员，
- **EN** + unfreeze 5.5 I declare this Appreciation Ceremony ... commenced. Free the zeroth block!
  **CN** + unfreeze 5.5 我宣布，本次鉴赏仪式…… 正式开始。解冻零号区块！
  *导演注释：Becoming more formal.*
- **EN** We begin with four points.
  **CN** 我们从四个点开始。
- **EN** Simple points of dimension zero.
  **CN** 简单纯粹的零维之点。

### `end1_dimension_1`

- **EN** It's quite nice having unexpected Visitors, though we invited neither of you.
  **CN** 虽然你们两个都不是我们邀请来的，但有不速之客， 感觉还挺不错。
- **EN** We'll have to figure out how that's happening. Decay-related, possibly. Regardless, I wouldn't mind more of it.
  **CN** 我们得弄清这些访客是怎么来的。可能和侵蚀有关。 不管怎样，我倒不介意再多来几位访客。

- **EN** She's not from your world?
  **CN** 她不是来自你的世界？

- **EN** Then by what method did she get here? Could matter a lot.
  **CN** 那她是怎么来到这里的？这件事可能非常重要。
- **EN** But... back to the formalities.
  **CN** 不过……还是先回到仪式上。

- **EN** Two points, connected, determine a line.
  **CN** 两点相连，定为一线。
- **EN** Each line defines a continuum, a dimension, where before there was none.
  **CN** 每条线都定义出一片连续空间， 也就是此前并不存在的一个维度。
- **EN** + unfreeze Trace the lines and connect the worlds.
  **CN** + unfreeze 循线而行，连接诸界。

### `end1_dimension_2`

- **EN** How do you find it, watching an Endgame from this perspective?
  **CN** 从这个视角观看终局，感觉如何？
- **EN** Nobody understands all of it!
  **CN** 没人能把一切都弄明白！
- **EN** Let's continue.
  **CN** 我们继续。
- **EN** Three points define three lines that trace a boundary,

- **EN** and thus delimit an area, a spread of two dimensions.

- **EN** + unfreeze Four worlds generate four such spreads, and only four.
  **CN** + unfreeze 四个世界会生成四片这样的区域，而且只能是四片。

### `end1_dimension_3`

- **EN** Do you remember these Solutions, from back when it was your turn?
  **CN** 当年轮到你解谜时的这些解法，你还记得吗？

- **EN** Good. Good.
  **CN** 很好。很好。

- **EN** Four points, taken all at once, give rise to dimension 3, unique.
  **CN** 四点同时用上，便能确定唯一的三维空间。
- **EN** A pyramid standing before the dawn of time, its volume hides mysteries;
  **CN** 一座立于时间黎明之前的棱锥，其中蕴藏奥秘；

### `end1_dimension_4`

- **EN** Your journey here complete.
  **CN** 你在这里的旅程至此结束。
  *导演注释：Formal / ritual reading.*
- **EN** what you've seen will live within you.
  **CN** 你所见的一切都会留在你心中。
  *导演注释：Todo: Revise this line.*
- **EN** So mote it be.
  **CN** 诚愿如此。
  *导演注释：Introductory*
- **EN** Sokoban, thank you for doing such a nice job. This was fun.
  **CN** 守库人，谢谢你把活儿干得这么好。刚才挺有意思。
  *导演注释：Confirmatory / concluding*
- **EN** + unfreeze Be seeing you.
  **CN** 回头见。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们来到这里，是为了观察不同事物如何组合。 刚开始探索这种仓库时，你看到的是一个个具体的物体：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 镜子会按固定规则，把物体传送到镜面另一侧、距离相等的位置。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你先观察它的基本规律，再用它解几个谜题，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 观察它在各种组合下如何与其他事物互动；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但经过一段时间的练习，你会渐渐觉得镜子的运作规律很自然。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 比如一颗飞石：它掠过水面，要么跳上岸，要么沉入水中； 然后你再来一次：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 先解些基础谜题，不断熟悉飞石的规律，直到能够运用自如。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 当我们把镜子和飞石放在一起时，你会看到那股神秘力量开始发挥作用：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这两件物体一旦靠近，就会彼此作用，变得更加复杂。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 飞石惯于朝一个方向前进。现在，镜子对飞石的作用
- **EN** 
  **CN** 就不再局限于位置，也会改变它的运动方向。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这样就会产生一些有趣的新局面， 我们再通过设计新谜题把它们呈现出来。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你看，镜子能改变飞石的运动方向。要是它恰好转向竖直方向， 而重力又只沿这个方向起作用，会怎么样？
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们来试试看。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这种影响是双向的：飞石反过来也会改变镜子—— 你以后也许就会看到。
  *导演注释：Enthusiastic; this is the end of this line of thought.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 现在，如果你想直观地看到可能性是如何绽放的，不妨想想： 每个世界起初都包含不止一个物体。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 为方便说明，我们先假设每个世界里只有两样东西。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 一个世界里有盗贼和哥布林； 另一个世界里有宝石和一道能让人穿墙的光束。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们把这些关系画出来。 用一个点代表盗贼，再用一个点代表哥布林，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 再用一条线把这两个点连起来，表示盗贼与哥布林之间的互动。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 另一个世界也是如此：宝石和光束之间连着一条线。 我给你画出来。目前只有两条线，记住这个数字“二”。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 现在，我们把这两个世界里的四种物体，全都放到同一个地方。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 原本就在同一个世界里的物体，互动方式并没有改变。 我们之前已经见过它们如何互动，现在也还是一样。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但来自不同世界、原本没有交集的任意两样东西， 现在也会产生互动了。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这里出现了四种新的互动：宝石能与盗贼互动， 也能与哥布林互动，图上就多了两条连线。
  *导演注释：We are about to use the word "interact" a lot.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 穿透光束也能与盗贼、哥布林分别互动，图上又多了两条连线。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我把它们画成红色。没必要反过来再画一遍，因为宝石与盗贼的互动，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 和盗贼与宝石的互动是一回事，至少对我们这里的讨论来说如此。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以一开始只有两条线，现在却有六条，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 没有加入任何新物体；我们只是把原有的东西组合了起来。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们关注的，正是事物彼此结合并随之扩展的那一刻；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这正是我们建造这些仓库的全部意义。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们之所以对此感兴趣，是因为这是现实的一个基本特征：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 现实世界不断把已有的元素组合起来， 创造出前所未见的事物。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们所选出的精品中，还有一部作品从道家传统中汲取灵感，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 不过，是从数学的角度来解读其中的思想。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你应该记得：道生一，一生二，依此类推。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以，我们想探讨这样一个过程：从还辨认不出任何具体事物，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 发展到能辨认出一个，再到许多个。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 那么，来看看“划界”这个概念：也就是划出界线，把事物彼此区分开。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这是对“创造”的另一种理解。我说的是“还辨认不出任何具体事物”，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 但别把它想成物理上的虚无。 它更像是无处不在的潜能。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 一个浑然一体、没有任何分别的整体，自然也不需要“划界”。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可一旦说有了“一”，就必然会产生这样一道界限：
  *导演注释：Proceeding with the main story.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 把这个“一”，与一切并非这个“一”的东西区分开。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 为了用数学表达这套想法，那位作者从所谓的“第一次划界”入手。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这道“界限”意味着现在我们可以得出两种概念：A与非A。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 一旦有了边界，也就有了跨越边界的可能。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你可以越过边界，从A进入非A；也可以反过来，从非A回到A。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 换到人类更熟悉的尺度，也许是从活着到死去，再从死去到活着； 从满足到厌倦，再从厌倦到满足。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这种跨越界限的过程，也能用数学符号表示。一个直角标记就有两层意思：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 既表明有一道界限，也表明“我跨过了它”。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 接下来，我们用两条规则，对写下的这些符号进行变换。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 如果你跨过去，再跨回来，最后仍在起点，就和一直没动一样。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 反过来也一样。凡是你停在原地的地方，也就是没写任何东西的地方，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 都可以改写成“跨过去，再跨回来”，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 写出来，就像一个直角标记里面套着另一个。

  **CN** 而把同一道界线重复划两遍，效果和只划一遍相同……
- **EN**

- **EN**

- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 很好。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你可以不断延伸这棵树的分支，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 通过一步步改写，用一样事物替换另一样，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 只要你有耐心，就可以一直扩展这个表达式。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而“道”有的是耐心。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们这么随手涂画，竟然创造出了无数复杂精巧的形态，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而这一切，不过源于“越界”和“重复划界”。
- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 一片“可数无穷”的丛林。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这能让你窥见我们在这里研究的一小部分内容。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 好好想想，我们很快再谈。
- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 你也许会觉得，既然要认真做这件事，就该把所有发现都展示出来。 其实恰恰相反，
- **EN**

- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们人啊，眼界有限，能力也弱。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 就连我这样经过人为强化的人也不例外。 （当然，只要你打好手里的牌，将来你也能得到强化。）
- **EN** 
  **CN** 人的注意力终究有限。所以，等我们完成设计上的探索， 找到这些本身很有意思、却未必能直接吸引人的闪光点之后，
  *导演注释：She is "strengthened", and Leamington will before long be "strengthened", raising intelligence, ability to focus, improving health, etc.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 还有一项不容小觑的工作，也是我们这门艺术的重要一环：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 那就是琢磨怎样呈现这些想法，好让访客乐在其中。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为如果大家只觉得无聊或一头雾水，就看不见其中的妙处。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 所以，我们得从中挑出有意思、能让人满足、让人愉悦的素材。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 有趣的一大标志，就是惊喜：
- **EN** 
  **CN** 作为玩家，你起初看不出解题的方向， 觉得谜题难以捉摸，甚至根本无解；
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可忽然灵光一闪，你找到了答案。 那答案既新鲜，又有意思。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 那一刻，一切豁然开朗。 而就在那一刻，你也超越了原先的自己，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 因为你的思路开阔了，也有了以前不曾有过的独特领悟。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 那么，我们要如何创造这种惊喜的瞬间？
- **EN** 
  **CN** 如果一道谜题里的物体全是随机挑的， 你能用它给人制造惊喜吗？
  *导演注释：after a pause*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 毕竟，没人能预料到那些事物会形成这种特定组合！
  *导演注释：A rhetorical question to which the answer is a predetermined No.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可一箱破烂没什么乐趣可言；组合得太随机， 只会让人觉得乱成一团。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而且，随机组合的东西看着其实都差不多。 一个接一个看下去也不会有什么新鲜感。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可反过来，如果我们设计的布局太单薄、太平淡，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 你一眼就能看个明白，自然也就没什么惊喜了。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们的作品必须刚好贴着访客当前的理解上限，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 让访客一开始还看不透其中的巧思，发现不了那份惊喜， 但经过努力，就能自己想明白。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 真正的乐趣，来自这样的惊喜： 不是发现“迷宫里该往哪边走”这种随意设定的答案，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 而是让你发现，自己所处的世界竟还有从前不知道的重要一面。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这一事实，在沉星之序之外几乎没人认真看待。
  *导演注释："the world you're in" meaning either these simulated game worlds or the real world.*
- **EN** 
  **CN** “意外”这东西，似乎有点特别。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 要是世上的事全都可以预料，那还有什么事需要你去琢磨、去决定的？ 因为你已经无所不知。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可那样的世界也太简单了，没什么趣味， 更不可能有人类存在。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可如果世界过于随机、混乱—— 我们根本不知道接下来会发生什么。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 当你甚至不知道什么才算食物时， 又要怎样获得食物、栖身之所和安全，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 可就算弄清了，世界又变化得那么快， 你刚得出的结论转眼就不成立了呢？
- **EN** 
  **CN** 幸好，我们的世界介于两者之间。 它依照规则运转，产生的结果也有规律可循。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这些规律既可辨识，也可预测。 所以，我们人类练就了一整套本领，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这些本领都与形成预期有关，也就是预测接下来会发生什么。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 事情一旦不照我们预想的来，我们就会感到意外。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 有些意外让人惊喜，有些却会带来恐惧，甚至要了我们的命。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们常常确实会从意外中获得乐趣， 有人会认为这不过是一种生物学上的
- **EN** 
  **CN** 功能罢了。但我们中的许多人，反而把它看作一个深不可测的谜。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 这话说到这里没错，不过事情可能还有更深一层。
  *导演注释：In Conclusion*
- **EN** 
  **CN** 我们之前说过，现实世界不断把已有的元素组合起来， 创造出前所未见的事物。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 也许，这一切的意义，恰恰就在于看到新结果时的那份惊喜。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 也许宇宙一直在创造自己的乐趣，而我们区区人类，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 当我们解开某道谜题、因豁然开朗而感到片刻喜悦时，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 也许只是感受到了宇宙中那份原本就有的喜悦，与之产生共鸣，
- **EN** 
  **CN** 短暂地窥见现实的本质。
- **EN** 
  **CN** 也许，我们可以学着让这种共鸣变得更强。

### `audiolog_sky_complexification_1` （无官方中译）

- **EN** We're here to look at the way things combine. At first, when exploring a Warehouse like this one, you see specific objects:
- **EN** A Mirror behaves in a certain way, teleporting objects to equidistant positions.
- **EN** You observe the basic behavior, you do some solving with it,
- **EN** see how it interacts in various permutations;
- **EN** with time and practice the Mirror's behavior becomes natural to you.
- **EN** A Skipping Stone: it skips over water, it will land on shore or it will sink, and you do it again:
- **EN** basic solving, working with the stone's behavior until it becomes natural.
- **EN** When we put the Mirror and the Skipping Stone together, you see the mysterious force begin to act:
- **EN** the two objects, brought into proximity, *complexify* with each other.
- **EN** The Stone likes to travel in one direction, so *now* when the Mirror acts, it acts
- **EN** not just on the Stone's position, but this direction as well.
- **EN** That creates new situations of interest, which we illustrate by making new puzzles.
- **EN** Oh, look, the Mirror *changes* the direction. What if the new direction happens to be vertical, the one axis affected by Gravity?
- **EN** Let's find out.
- **EN** This complexification goes both ways. The Stone can make some things happen to the Mirror as well -- as you may see eventually.
  *导演注释：Enthusiastic; this is the end of this line of thought.*

### `audiolog_sky_complexification_2` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Now if you want to visualize the blooming of possibility, it helps to consider that each world begins with *more* than one object.
- **EN** For simplicity, to start, imagine each world just had *two* things.
- **EN** One world has a Thief and a Goblin; another world has Gems and a Beam That Lets You Walk Through Walls.
- **EN** We draw these relationships. For Thief we draw a dot, and Goblin is a dot,
- **EN** and the interaction between Thief and Goblin is a line that connects them.
- **EN** Same in the other world: a line connects Gem and Beam. I'll draw this out for you. It's only two lines so far; keep that number two in mind.
- **EN** Now, we put all four of these objects, from both worlds, into the same place.
- **EN** With the objects that were already old friends, nothing happens; we already saw how they work, and it stays the same.
- **EN** But any two objects from *different* worlds, who have never met, want to play together now.
- **EN** Here, we see four new interactions: Gem interacts with Thief, and also with Goblin; that's two more lines.
  *导演注释：We are about to use the word "interact" a lot.*
- **EN** And Through Beam interacts with Thief, and also Goblin; that's two more lines as well.
- **EN** I'll draw those in red. You don't have to go the other way, because Gem with Thief
- **EN** is the same as Thief with Gem, at least for our purposes here.
- **EN** So we started with just two lines. But now we have six lines,
- **EN** without adding any new objects. We merely brought together things that we already had.
- **EN** It is this moment of intercombining, and the resultant expansion,
- **EN** that we watch carefully. It's the whole reason we build these Warehouses.
- **EN** We're interested in this, because this is a basic aspect of Reality:
- **EN** Reality is always combining the elements we *already have* and producing things *never before seen*.

### `audiolog_sky_laws_of_form` （无官方中译）

- **EN** There's another work from our canon, that takes inspiration
- **EN** from the Daoist tradition, but puts a mathematical spin on it.
- **EN** You'll recall that first the Dao creates or gives rise to One, One creates Two, and so on.
- **EN** So we want to look at the idea of going from nothing that is identifiable,
- **EN** to one identifiable thing, to many identifiable things.
- **EN** So let's consider the idea of Distinction -- lines being drawn to distinguish things from each other.
- **EN** It's a different picture of creation. I said "nothing that is identifiable",
- **EN** but don't imagine this as physical nothingness. More like an all-pervading potential.
- **EN** An undifferentiated whole has no need for distinction.
- **EN** But once we say there is One, this necessarily involves a distinction:
  *导演注释：Proceeding with the main story.*
- **EN** between that One, and whatever that One is not.
- **EN** To model this mathematically, the author began with what he called the First Distinction.
- **EN** This Distinction means that now we have two things, A and Not A.
- **EN** Once you have a boundary, this admits the possibility of crossing the boundary.
- **EN** You can cross from A to Not A, or from Not A to A.
- **EN** On a more human scale, perhaps Alive to Dead, then back to Alive. Contented to Bored, then back to Contented.
- **EN** You can write crossings mathematically, where a single right-angled mark both
- **EN** states that there is a distinction, and also says "I crossed it".
- **EN** And then, you can introduce two rules for manipulating what you wrote.
- **EN** If you cross and cross back again, you end up where you started -- that's the same as staying still.
- **EN** Likewise in reverse. Anywhere you stood still, in other words wrote nothing,
- **EN** you can replace this with crossing and then crossing back,
- **EN** which looks like one right-angled mark inside another.
- **EN** And marking down the same distinction twice is the same as marking it once...
- **EN** you are just saying the same thing twice, redundantly.
  *导演注释：Shifting to the next part of the topic.*
- **EN** (This looks like two right-angled marks next to each other.)
- **EN** So anywhere you wrote a distinction, you can write it twice, three times, however many you want.
- **EN** With just these two rules of rewriting, a tree can blossom that is huge, as large as you like.
- **EN** You know what I mean by a tree, in the mathematical sense?
- **EN** Good.
- **EN** You can keep growing the branches of this tree,
- **EN** replacing one thing with another via incremental rewrites,
- **EN** expanding the expression for as long as your patience permits.
- **EN** And the Dao is very patient.
- **EN** With all our doodling, we've just created
- **EN** a huge proliferation of elaborate forms. All from just crossings and restatements.
- **EN** These forms we wrote, when reduced again, all boil down to Distinction or No Distinction --
- **EN** but the tree structure you can create has a huge number of possibilities.
- **EN** A countably infinite jungle.
- **EN** This gives you some insight into a small part of what we study here.
- **EN** Think on this, and we'll speak again soon.

### `audiolog_sky_play_of_form_2` （无官方中译）

- **EN** As we've discussed, our Universe produces a tremendous proliferation of material.
- **EN** We study these productions and we show what we find to others.
- **EN** It may seem that, to take this activity seriously, we must show everything. But the contrary is true,
- **EN** because much of the material we discover can seem dry, repetitive, obscure. Hard to appreciate.
- **EN** We people, we are short-sighted and weak.
- **EN** Even artificially strengthened people, like myself. (And you, of course, if you play your cards properly.)
- **EN** People have limited attention spans. So, *after* we perform our design excursions and find these dispassionate nuggets of interestingness,
  *导演注释：She is "strengthened", and Leamington will before long be "strengthened", raising intelligence, ability to focus, improving health, etc.*
- **EN** it is a significant portion of our Art, which is not to be underestimated,
- **EN** that we work out how to frame the ideas to make them entertaining to our Visitors.
- **EN** Because if everyone is just bored, or confused, they don't *see*.
- **EN** So we sift for material that is interesting, satisfying, delightful.

### `audiolog_sky_play_of_form_2_surprise` （无官方中译）

- **EN** A primary indicator of interest is surprise:
- **EN** as a Player, you didn't know where a puzzle was going; it seemed mysterious, or impossible;
- **EN** but then BAM, with an insight, you see the answer, and it's something really interesting and new.
- **EN** There's a moment of clarity. In that moment, you become someone greater,
- **EN** because your mind is now enlarged; it now holds something special that it didn't before.
- **EN** So, how do we create moments of surprise?
- **EN** Can you surprise someone with a puzzle full of objects chosen completely at random?
  *导演注释：after a pause*
- **EN** After all, nobody could expect that exact combination of things!
  *导演注释：A rhetorical question to which the answer is a predetermined No.*
- **EN** But there's nothing delightful about a box of junk; compositions that are *too* random feel like a mess.
- **EN** Also, random things feel the same as each other, so a sequence of them offers nothing new as it goes along.
- **EN** On the other hand, if the arrangement we make is too spare, too flat,
- **EN** you can see everything about it on the first look. Clearly, there will be no surprise there either.
- **EN** Our composition needs to be on the edge of the Visitor's understanding,
- **EN** so that the trick of the arrangement, its surprise, is beyond him at first, but then, with effort, he can pull himself across the gap.
- **EN** Real enjoyment comes when the surprise is not arbitrary, like which direction to go in a maze,
- **EN** but rather, if it reveals something salient about the world you're in that you didn't already know.
- **EN** This particular fact is scarcely taken seriously outside our Order.
  *导演注释："the world you're in" meaning either these simulated game worlds or the real world.*

### `audiolog_sky_play_of_form_3` （无官方中译）

- **EN** Surprise seems to be special.
- **EN** If the world were completely predictable, there would be nothing to figure out or decide, as everything would be known to you.
- **EN** But, such a world would be too simple to be interesting. Certainly it could contain no humans.
- **EN** If the world were *too* random and chaotic -- we wouldn't have any idea what could happen next.
- **EN** How do you get food, shelter, safety, when you have no idea what *is* food,
- **EN** and if you do figure that out, the world changes so quickly that your discovery becomes invalid in the next moment?
- **EN** Fortunately, we're somewhere in the middle. The world operates according to rules, and those rules generate patterned outcomes.
- **EN** Those patterns are recognizable and predictable. So we humans have developed an extensive set of skills,
- **EN** that are all about having expectations. Predicting what we think will happen next.
- **EN** And when our expectations are defied, the result is *surprise*.
- **EN** Sometimes surprise can be very delightful; sometimes it is horrifying and we die.
- **EN** That we often *do* find surprise enjoyable, some would believe is a mere biological
- **EN** function. But many of us regard it instead as a deep mystery.
- **EN** I think that's true, insofar as it goes. But consider there may be more to it.
  *导演注释：In Conclusion*
- **EN** We've said that Reality is always combining the elements we *already have* and producing things *never before seen*.
- **EN** Perhaps the *point* of this involves surprise at seeing new results.
- **EN** Perhaps the Universe is engaged in a constant process of generating its own delight, and we mere humans,
- **EN** when we solve some puzzle and feel a moment of joy in understanding,
- **EN** perhaps we are just tapping into that background delight, harmonizing with it,
- **EN** experiencing a brief insight into the fundamental nature of reality.
- **EN** Perhaps strengthening that connection is a skill we can learn.

### `paradise_lost_invocation_2` （无官方中译）

### `psalm_139`

- **EN** If I ascend up into heaven, thou art there:
  **CN** 我若升到天上，祢在那里；
- **EN** if I make my bed in hell, behold, thou art there.
  **CN** 我若在阴间下榻，看哪，祢也在那里。
  *导演注释：Being very old, she has a great deal of life experience, many triumphs and tragedies that she is reflecting upon.*
- **EN** If I take the wings of the morning, and dwell in the uttermost parts of the sea;
  **CN** 我若展开清晨的翅膀，飞到海的一角居住；
- **EN** Even there shall thy hand lead me, and thy right hand shall hold me.
  **CN** 就是在那里，祢的手必引导我，祢的右手也必扶持我。
- **EN** If I say, Surely the darkness shall cover me; even the night shall be light about me.
  **CN** 我若说：“黑暗必定遮蔽我”，连我周围的黑夜也必成为光明。
- **EN** Yea, the darkness hideth not from thee; but the night shineth as the day:
  **CN** 黑暗也不能遮蔽我，使祢不见；黑夜却如白昼发亮：
- **EN** the darkness and the light are both alike to thee.
